With the second volume complete, I am delighted to see its characters established one by one and embraced by everyone. Ending matters decisively with 0-17 was not out of the question. On one hand, it showed how formidable the Churches are; they have remained the ruling powers for so long for good reason. On the other hand, that was not the ending itself, merely a continuation that links what came before to what follows and points toward later developments. I will say no more, lest I spoil anything.

Finally, in writing novels, I increasingly pursue simplicity and penetration. Whatever can be described accurately with the plainest, simplest words should not be drawn out or smothered in ornament. Emotion should accumulate step by step through calm, objective prose, then, at precisely the right moment, the most exact and simple language should pierce the barrier and strike the softest place in your heart.

Although many problems remain, I am still on the journey.

Of course, the nature of serialized web fiction means I can achieve this only in the most important chapters, where it is needed most. It is impossible to write every chapter that way. Many things can only be revised when I am older and have time to spare. In general, my vocabulary is extensive but unfamiliar to me; words accumulated little by little are useless if they do not come naturally. When writing habitually, one draws from the list of words most readily available in the mind, so repetition is difficult to avoid.

Similarly, in trying to present the clearest, most immediate, and most important images in my mind, I sometimes misuse adjectives or attempt to coin expressions. This still requires exploration and improvement.

The above is the technical summary of the second volume.

Writing is arduous and torturous, but once the words are written, receiving praise is delightful; I feel exceptionally happy and fulfilled. Recently, everyone has praised me for being full of enthusiasm.

Gentlemen, I like to write novels!

I like to tell you stories!

I like creating vivid, fully realized characters one by one!

I like to show you an interesting and novel world!

Writing novels is really a joy!

Well, it is astonishing that today's highest subscription count on the Qidian platform is 53,000, with every chapter above 41,000. The twenty-four-hour subscription count is 27,000 to 28,000, and the latest chapters have officially surpassed 31,000. This shows that reader retention is high, that most who begin reading continue to follow the story, and that all of you are full of love!

The recommendation votes have also remained in the top fifteen, and the monthly tickets in the top five. I am too lazy to manipulate any figures. Therefore, every vote, every reward, and every subscription is filled with love!

Gentlemen, the second volume is over. I want to ask for praise and a monthly ticket!

I am asking for monthly ticketssssssss!

Finally, according to convention, we will take two days off: Monday and Tuesday. We will return at the usual time, 12:30 p.m. on Wednesday.

What? You are asking about Sunday? I didn't hear you, I didn't hear you... All right, I am joking. Let me rest for half a day after such a long volume.

The third volume, "Traveler", please look forward to it.

Chapter 483: New Identity

The sky outside the window was dark, but not with the murky fog Klein was accustomed to. The sea breeze swept inland, blowing away the smog and arranging the clouds into various shapes that reflected the reddish-gold sunlight.

This was Pritz Harbor, the largest and busiest port in the Loen Kingdom.

Wearing a light vest and white shirt, Klein stood by the window and watched the outside world for a while until his pocket watch urged him to return to the mahogany table.

In the warmth of the fireplace, he picked up a black, round fountain pen, unfolded a letter, and slowly wrote:

Dear Mr. Azik,

Forgive me for not writing to you until today, but for the past few days, I've been wandering through Backlund, immersed in the devastation inflicted on this great city by recent events. If we were ordinary people, perhaps we would've been covered with white cloth and carried to the crematorium, eventually coming to rest in a tiny niche...

I've waited quite a while and finally found an opportunity to retrieve what belongs to me. This includes the Card of Blasphemy I promised you. In addition, I'll have the messenger bring you another item: a copper whistle that can summon a messenger. It came from a chance encounter involving an elder who crawled out of his coffin. I'm sure you're puzzled after reading this, since my description also seems to point to you. That is precisely what puzzles me.

... This is exactly what happened. I suspect that the original owner of the copper whistle is a member of the Numinous Episcopate that tries to revive Death. Furthermore, his level isn't low. Perhaps you'll be able to tell something from this copper whistle...

Before leaving Backlund, I will write to the Machinery Hivemind to describe the massive underground ruin where you fought Ince Zangwill. I hope that they will be able to figure out the truth with the help of this information.

After conducting a roundabout test, I've confirmed that they bear no enmity toward either of us for the time being. If you're in trouble, perhaps you can try seeking their help.

Finally, I have one more question. Is there any way to remove the residual mental corruption of a Beyonder characteristic that has already solidified?

... I am about to set sail. I wish you a smooth journey in finding your memories and a safe journey for myself as well.

Your student and friend, Klein Moretti.

Putting down the pen and reading it once more, Klein folded the letter and stuffed it into the envelope along with the Black Emperor card and the copper whistle left behind by the suspected Numinous Episcopate member.

When he was done, he picked up the copper whistle Mr. Azik had given him and summoned the messenger by blowing into it.

The messenger was still nearly four meters tall, made purely of white bones, its eye sockets burning with black fire. However, Klein's spiritual intuition told him that this was another messenger.

Sighing secretly, Klein raised his arm and placed the letter into the messenger's lowered palm.

The messenger lowered its head to take a look before rapidly disintegrating into a torrent of bones that plunged into the ground.

Seeing this, Klein lightly tapped his right molar and deactivated his Spirit Vision.

He returned his gaze to the table, where a pale yellow identification card lay. It was required to purchase passage on any ship through official channels.

For this, he had specially gone to Sharron and obtained a new identity through her circle.

This identity belonged to a bounty hunter, a madman eager to seek adventure and riches at sea. In accordance with Klein's wishes, his name was Gehrman Sparrow.

"A hunter of evil..." Klein whispered, putting away a series of documents for his new identity.

Shortly after, he drew the curtains, took four steps counterclockwise, and went above the fog.

There was still some time before the Tarot Gathering, so Klein quickly retrieved Creeping Hunger and wore it on his hand.

Closing his eyes, he tried to sense each and every twisted, illusory soul. He attempted to release the Faceless.

In the real world, Creeping Hunger would happily consume this offering and spit out the corresponding Beyonder characteristic, but above the gray fog, it didn't dare act rashly. It allowed the Faceless's soul to leave the glove and appear beside the long bronze table.

It was a middle-aged man with a blurry face. His twisted and painful feelings seemed to dissipate quite a bit.

With difficulty, he bowed at Klein, who was leaning back in his chair. His figure gradually turned dim, almost falling beneath the gray fog at any moment.

In the majestic palace, Klein could directly "communicate" without any additional rituals prepared; thus, he extended his spirituality and stabilized the man before saying in a low voice, "Do you know where there are live mermaids?"

The man answered in a distant voice, "Apart from those kept by the Church of Evernight, they can only be found by sailing from the Gargas Archipelago toward the Sonia Sea for at least a week. That was my destination."

So he's also a Faceless seeking to advance... In order to seek out mermaids, he had taken the risk to go out to sea. However, he somehow died at the hands of Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos in the end... The Church of the Goddess rears quite a number of mermaids? Klein suddenly asked in realization, "Which organization did you belong to? Or should I say, where did your potion formula come from?"

The blurry-faced middle-aged man's body suddenly trembled. It was only two seconds later that he spoke up.

"The Secret Order. I belong to the Secret Order."

Secret Order? Doesn't the Secret Order rear their own mermaids? Klein hesitated for a moment before asking, "Have you ever seen your leader, Zaratul?"

The illusory and transparent Faceless was silent at first, but then he shouted with a sharp voice, "I have!

"H-he's abnormal! He's an undying monster!"

As he spoke, his figure became increasingly thin, almost on the verge of dissipating.

As expected, Zaratul is still alive! Just what had happened for a Secret Order member to be so afraid of him? More accurately, I should use "Him"... Klein quickly asked about another key issue, "Apart from the treasures left behind by the Antigonus family and what your Secret Order has, is there anywhere I can get the High-Sequence Beyonder formulas for the Seer pathway?"

The Faceless turned increasingly transparent and more illusory. He finally left the words: "The Church of Evernight... Cathedral of

Serenity..."

The Holy Cathedral... Klein repeated the words as he silently watched the Faceless's Spirit Body attain complete liberation. The Cathedral of Serenity was the headquarters of the Church of Evernight, also known to the Nighthawks as the Holy Cathedral.

Alger and the others had similar thoughts as her. They all thought that the repeated cycles of life were close to a miracle that could only be destroyed by a deity. Fortunately, the master of the Tarot Club was a deity. Mr. Fool was a deity!

At that moment, Derrick looked across the table and said sincerely, "Miss Magician, we encountered some unexpected circumstances on our return to the City of Silver. Yes, I believe I'm no longer under surveillance."

She had sold the Mirror Dragon's eyes to Miss Justice and had received 1,000 pounds in cash.

After informing her teacher, Dorian, of the exact results, her honesty had been praised and approved. Eater.

It will take me at least two weeks to digest the rest of the potion. I'm not in a hurry... Looking diagonally across the table, Fors said, "Mr. Hanged Man, there's news of the Dragon-Eyed Sea Condor you require. 2,200 pounds."

Dorian had given a reference price of 2,000 pounds. On the one hand, Fors had allowed room for bargaining, and on the other hand, wished to earn a little more.

2,200 pounds... Alger couldn't help but frown.

His financial situation wasn't looking good after he purchased the Wind-blessed potion formula. Although he still had some secret savings, they were either of use elsewhere, or he hadn't been able to find a buyer for a long time.

Phew. He secretly let out a sigh and calmly said, "Very well, but I'll need some time to collect the funds. In addition, yours is too expensive, I have many channels to get cheaper ones. 1,900 pounds. That's the highest price I can accept."

"Deal!

Sanguine? Alger was taken aback at first, but he soon relaxed his brows.

Sanguine? The World was late by a second before giving a normal, necessary response.

Chapter 487: The Growing Tarot Club

Wait, they keep mentioning the City of Silver. Where exactly is it? Why haven't I heard of it... After the retort, Emlyn realized that he had apparently overlooked something important.

If no one stops them, I think they'll argue about this until the gathering ends... Although Little Sun is honest, sincere, and reserved, he has a very stubborn and persistent personality. Use potions to inherit an inheritance to prevent the Sanguine's powers from being lost... Emlyn suddenly felt that Mr. Hanged Man was very reasonable. Hence, he asked, "What's the price?"

With such a large sum of money, I can buy a few puppets that I've been eyeing for a long time, and I can even get some new clothes for all my dolls! This was what Emlyn learned from Sherlock Moriarty. He felt that this was very good and reasonable, because a middleman needed to be paid!

To me, the most important thing is to advance to Sequence 7... Emperor Roselle once said that any problem that can be solved with money isn't a problem... Audrey's recently heavy mood improved.

At the same time, she received gifts of more than 20,000 pounds in jewelry, horses, hounds, paintings, etc. Most of them came from her mother, two elder brothers, and other members of the Hall family.

As for the cash, she didn't have much. It was only 5,000 pounds.

Of course, Audrey didn't dare reveal her intention to aim for Sequence 7. She could still only use her pocket money and savings.

Well, I'll be able to pay off my debt to Glaint this month. Next month, I can pay 2,000 pounds to Mr. Fool's Blessed... I won't be short of money at all... Audrey thought cheerfully. Very rich... Emlyn took a moment to react before asking in puzzlement, "How do we complete the deal?"

She was very proactive in obtaining The Fool's consent, conjuring the corresponding ritual requirements.

When Emlyn received the two goatskin parchments, he stared at them in a daze. The way they traded was beyond his imagination.

Watching the transactions between the members, Klein was very pleased. As time passed, the channels and resources available to the Tarot Club would continue to grow. Miss Justice represents the Psychology Alchemists, the Loen aristocrats, and the power of money... Mr. Hanged Man represents the Church of Storms and the resources at sea. Father will definitely agree to let me buy a mystical item with tolerable side effects! Audrey added inwardly, feeling exceptionally relaxed.

Chapter 488: Living Expenses

In a sense, the Beyonder characteristic The World entrusted to me has already been sold. The finished item has a definite buyer... I can simplify the process and increase my profit. I can receive a commission from The World and also earn more from Miss Justice. The only capital I need to put up is the Artisan's fabrication fee. Of course, I have to consider whether the Artisan will be willing to take the risk...

Many thoughts flashed through his mind as he quickly weighed the pros and cons and finally came to a decision.

"No problem, I'll take care of this matter." He looked at Miss Justice and felt that she was emitting a blinding light of gold.

During the Second Epoch, there was no distinction between Hermes and ancient Hermes. A person named Hermes really does appear in the City of Silver's history... Klein nodded inwardly and said, "What a pity. I wanted to ask you a favor."

The ruins of Hermes? This old gentleman was still alive a hundred or two hundred years ago... Hermes is a member of the Twilight Hermit Order? The Psychology Alchemists might have something to do with this mysterious organization? Audrey was stunned before she let out a sigh of relief.

Fortunately, I have the Tarot Club and Mr. Fool backing me. Otherwise, I would have no way of knowing and no way to guard against... Once again, she gratefully praised The Fool.

Chapter 489: A Suggestion That Was Rejected

After another round of conversation, Derrick suddenly asked, "Honorable Mr. Fool, what hand gesture should we ordinarily use to thank you?"

Hand gesture? Klein was momentarily stumped by Little Sun's question.

It had never occurred to him to imitate the Church of the Goddess's gesture of drawing a clockwise circle, or the Church of Storms's prayer gesture of striking the left side of one's chest with the right fist.

This is probably a fake god's last shred of integrity... he lampooned inwardly.

Seeing Mr. Fool smile silently as though waiting for her and the others to make suggestions, Audrey was suddenly struck by inspiration and raised a new question.

Her eyes sparkled as she looked around and said, "Everyone, should we design a secret gesture to indicate our identity? Our Tarot Club doesn't have many members at present, so there's no need to worry about us appearing at the same spot and ending up opposing each other due to various reasons. But in the future, it might be very difficult to avoid that. We need a set of unique 'signals,' so as to identify friend from foe."

I have an idea for that... Klein suddenly remembered the secret hand gestures he knew from his previous life, such as the hand-in-waistcoat.

Just as he was about to manipulate The World to make a suggestion, The Hanged Man opened his mouth.

"No, Miss Justice, I don't think it's a good idea.

"Compared to other secret organizations, our Tarot Club's most striking characteristic is that its members don't know one another. Even if... Uh--let me give an extreme example. Even if one of our members were captured and interrogated, they couldn't compromise the others unless someone could resist Mr. Fool and enter this place directly. And that is clearly impossible.

"Once we establish a full set of signals and passcodes, a traitor could allow an opposing faction to use them to ferret us out one by one.

"Are you that eager to flaunt your identities as Tarot Club members?"

"..." Audrey couldn't think of a rebuttal and stammered, "But..."

"It's reasonable to worry about the situation you mentioned, but we can avoid it through our regular weekly meetings, by creating a temporary gesture in advance, and by praying to Mr. Fool." Considering that she would be providing him with large amounts of gold, Alger's tone gradually softened.

We would then devise a temporary gesture to identify ourselves and abandon it immediately afterward. Uh, if something unexpected happens and we can't communicate in time, or if we have any suspicions, we can find an opportunity to pray to Mr. Fool and confirm whether another member is involved in the same event?"

"Something like that." Alger let out a silent breath.

At that moment, Derrick said with sudden understanding, "Sorry, I asked a foolish question. Mr. Fool never mentioned any gestures for thanking 'Him' because he was worried we might expose ourselves."

He immediately looked to the end of the long bronze table.

"Your wish steers our path."

I just thought of a special hand gesture... Klein chuckled and replied, "That's right."

He cast his gaze towards the other members and said in a composed manner, "Let us end today's gathering here."

"By Your will!" All the members apart from The Moon stood up at the same time.

Emlyn froze for a second before hurriedly standing and imitating the other members' bows.

A deep red light immediately appeared in front of his eyes, and he felt his body plummet slightly.

Soon after, his vision returned to normal and he saw dolls of various sizes in his room.

Phew... Only then did Emlyn White completely calm down and reflect on his first Tarot Gathering.

Apart from Mr. Fool, the other members don't seem too powerful. Could they be like me, chosen for various reasons? I have the Ancestor backing me, so who's backing them?

What a joke. I originally imagined The Sun to be a demigod who just escaped the pursuit of an angel. It turned out that he's only a Sequence 8 and seeking to advance to Sequence 7!

A child who doesn't know manners definitely isn't an adult yet! However, the City of Silver he mentioned and his experiences are very strange. I have to find an opportunity to ask Lord Nibbs--no, I should first ask Cosmi and my parents. I'll see whether they know anything about the City of Silver. Humph, how dare the City of Silver's history books distort the achievements of us Sanguine!

Miss Justice is from Backlund. She's very, very rich. Is she the daughter of some rich banker or the daughter of some noble? Perhaps she's a banker or noble herself...

Miss Magician looked at me very strangely. She must admire the lofty Sanguine. She doesn't speak much and didn't divulge much about herself. Yes, she's a quiet girl.

Mr. Hanged Man is a mature gentleman. He knows a lot and has a sublime character. He's willing to answer questions from new members and is willing to provide the corresponding help and information. It can be said that he's very popular. The Sun and The World are both willing to ask him questions and seek his help.

The World is quite an unlikable person. When he speaks, it sounds as though there's phlegm in his throat that he can't spit out. I would disdain even drinking such a person's blood. It would taste filthy... He's very introverted and good at hiding his emotions. Furthermore, he easily produced a Sequence 6 Beyonder characteristic and promised to provide a Psychiatrist Beyonder characteristic in two months... Very impressive!

As the details flashed through his mind, Emlyn realized that the Tarot Club appeared simple but was anything but. Furthermore, Mr. Fool rarely interfered with the course of the gathering.

"He" seems interested only in the so-called pages of Roselle's diary... Yet "He" is willing to provide His members with convenient services bordering on miracles. At this thought, Emlyn couldn't help feeling a little proud of his identity as The Moon.

Surveying the room of dolls, he recalled his own problem.

A relic worth 4,000 to 5,000 pounds. A chance to become a baron in a short period of time. This is really putting me in a difficult position...

At this moment, although Emlyn hadn't made his final decision yet, he felt the room grow darker, as though he were shouldering a heavy debt.

In the ancient palace above the fog.

Klein pressed the thumb and middle finger of his right hand to his temple and gently rubbed it.

He quickly sat upright in the silent palace and summoned a small badge from the junk pile to the long bronze table before him. The badge was only the size of an eyeball. Its surface bore symbols representing "fate" and "concealment." Klein had found it on Lanevus's body.

Its back was inscribed in ancient Hermes with the words "You can join if you have this item." It also provided the gathering's details: "4 January 1350, 8 p.m., Babur Valley."

The question Klein needed to consider now was whether he should take the badge to the Babur Valley tomorrow night to try his luck.

Frankly speaking, he was inclined not to delve deeper. Even though he had already advanced to Faceless and was able to put on a perfect disguise, he didn't want to take the risk, because he didn't know anything about the gathering.

A Magician never performs unprepared... He muttered softly as he took out a gold coin and held it between his fingers.

He picked up the badge with his other hand and muttered to himself, "It's risky to attend the gathering in Babur Valley."

After repeating this a few times, he finally flicked the gold coin.

The gold coin tumbled down and stood upright in the palm of his hand.

This meant that the divination had failed.

"As expected..." Klein wasn't surprised.

This isn't a matter of insufficient information, but a complete absence of it.

He sat there in silence, letting the gold coin weave between his fingers.

In the end, Klein overcame his curiosity and adventurous spirit and decided not to go.

But that doesn't mean that I can't get involved. Tomorrow at 8 p.m., hmm... He smiled faintly and returned to the real world.

4th January, 10:35 a.m.

Klein stood in front of a mahogany table and picked up a stack of bills.

There were five ten-pound notes and ten five-pound notes, for a total of one hundred pounds. This was the payment The Hanged Man had just delivered through a sacrifice.

The fifteen notes made Klein's wallet much fuller, and he was finally able to buy his ticket in peace.

Putting his wallet away, he picked up a thin human-skinned glove on the table and put it on his left hand.

One of Creeping Hunger's main features was its camouflage when not in use, which prevented most Beyonder methods from detecting it. Klein could switch between its original appearance, gloves of various colors, and the semblance of bare skin. This time, he chose a black glove.

To match it, Klein had specially prepared a separate black glove for his right hand.

Soon after, he placed the brown Biological Poison Bottle into a metal box, sealed it with a wall of spirituality, and stuffed it into the inside pocket of his clothes.

As for the Sun Brooch, as long as he wore it and carried it with him, it would create a hot summer feeling. Klein had no choice but to put it in his iron cigar case and hide it at the bottom of his suitcase.

9 purifying bullets, 15 demon-hunting bullets, 3 exorcism bullets... Klein took out his revolver and a cartridge box, silently counting as he loaded them.

Pa!

He closed the cylinder, inserted the revolver into his underarm holster, put on his black tweed coat and half top hat, picked up his cane and suitcase, and walked out of the hotel room.

Apart from the All-Black Eye and other items he couldn't use, which remained above the gray fog, he was fully armed.

Taking a carriage, Klein arrived at the Pritz Harbor Ticket Company located in White Rose Borough.

This company was located in a rather old three-story building. There was a wooden notice board on the door.

Klein walked over and stopped there. He casually glanced at the board which said:

"Things to Note:

"1. Maintain order. Lines are strictly enforced; 2. Do not urinate, defecate, or spit; 3. Find the guard in the lobby if there is a dispute; 4. Do not open canned wolf-fish in any of the rooms!"

Canned wolf-fish? What's that? Klein raised his eyebrows.

Chapter 490: Warning from a Former Sailor

The lobby of the Pritz Harbor Ticket Company was rather spacious, with seven ticketing windows, but there were already a dozen or so people lined up in front of each.

Klein glanced around but didn't immediately join the shortest line. Instead, he took two steps to the right and stopped before an upright brown board.

Many sheets of white paper were pasted on the wooden board, displaying information about the coming week's passenger ships, including their destinations, ports of call, and cabin prices.

Before Klein could take a closer look, a staff member came over, circled the second-class cabins on one listing in red, and marked them with a single phrase: "Sold out."

"So popular..." Klein sighed.

"Of course. Pritz Harbor is the kingdom's largest port. Countless people pass through here on their way to the Southern Continent and the colonial islands in search of opportunity," a middle-aged man beside the wooden board replied with obvious pride.

He was wearing a black bonnet and a black-and-white checkered uniform similar to that of the police, but he had no epaulets. All he had was a seagull badge hanging from his chest--exactly identical to the logo of the Pritz Harbor Ticket Company.

The middle-aged man's face, hands, and all his exposed skin were bronzed and rough, as though years of sea wind and sun had worked salt into his wrinkles.

Find the guard in the lobby if there's a dispute... This must be the guard... Klein recalled the notice at the door. He didn't mind the man striking up a conversation and smiled. "You seem to know this port very well?"

The middle-aged man replied smugly, "I was once a sailor in the kingdom's navy, whose main base is on Oak Island in Pritz Harbor. I served for fifteen years and spent a long time sailing these waters. If the war in East Balam hadn't ruined my health, I could've remained a sailor for another ten years! I know this port as well as I know my wife's body!"

A little cultured, but also a little vulgar... Klein chatted casually with him, intending to ask for news from the sea.

"You became a guard here after retiring?"

"No, I was packed off to night school for two whole years, both as a student and a gatekeeper. Holy Lord of Storms, can you imagine someone my age reciting lessons with a bunch of teenagers? And they learn and remember words faster than I do!" The guard's expression showed how painful he found the memory.

As he spoke, he patted his thigh and sighed.

"Unfortunately, my knees can't stand wet weather; otherwise, I would be a part-time teacher at night. Those kids will make you feel young, but I won't deny that it's because I want to earn more money. When you have a wife and four children, you have to realize that you have to support your family."

Sir, you talk a little too much... Maybe that's why the ticket company hired you as a guard... Klein smiled and didn't continue with the man's topic.

"I just read the notice at the door and saw that opening canned wolf-fish isn't permitted here. Frankly, I've never heard of such a thing."

The guard's expression suddenly became complicated.

He pinched his nose and said, "It's a food popular along the east coast of Feysac and in places like the Gargas Archipelago. It's wolf-fish pickled in salt with the blood left in, and the smell--the smell is very, very powerful. It stinks. It's disgusting!"

So it's an exotic food... Klein laughed and said, "But surely no one would deliberately eat canned food while waiting in line to buy a ticket, right?"

"No, you don't understand the temptation. Perhaps one day you will." Lingering fear crossed the guard's face. "Once, a barbarian from the north came here to buy a ticket. The lines were so long that the hall looked like a barrel packed with fish. He grew impatient and opened a can of wolf-fish. In less than ten seconds, only he and a few other fellows remained in the lobby."

This... this is a biological weapon... An ordinary version of my Biological Poison Bottle... Klein laughed.

"So he successfully bought a ticket, and a new rule was added to the notice outside?"

"It didn't work out as he expected. The lady and gentleman selling tickets fled as well. Heh heh, as you know, barbarians have even less sense than curly-haired baboons!" The guard chuckled. "When I was a sailor, there was a rumor that a group of pirates seized a merchant ship from Rolles. Ah, that's a city on Feysac's east coast. In short, the pirates eagerly opened their spoils, only to discover barrels filled to the brim with wolf-fish. Can you guess what happened? They fainted, vomited, and lost the ability to fight, while the crew collected the bounty on them."

"Good story." Klein tried not to laugh.

He returned his gaze to the notices on the wooden board and searched for a ship scheduled to depart on 5th January.

As a professional, he had already divined the most suitable dates for setting sail that week: the 5th and the 8th. Among the passenger ships bound for the Rorsted Archipelago, the Saint Havre and the White Agate suited him best.

There are still tickets, and the prices are about the same: four pounds for third-class, ten pounds for second-class, and thirty-five pounds for first-class... People who depend on the sea for survival more or less believe in the Lord of the Storms. Even in countries like Intis and Feysac, there are fishermen and crew who secretly believe in this forbidden deity, in a bid to be safe out at sea... The name Saint Havre originates from the Church of Storms. It has a certain background... Klein thought it over. He was inclined to choose the White Agate.

Not in a rush to make a decision, he turned to look at the guard.

"Do you know anything about the White Agate?"

The guard immediately smiled.

"Sir, you have a good eye. The White Agate is a steam-powered ship, but it also retains sails. Its maximum speed is 16 knots.

"Also, the captain is very experienced. He was once the boatswain of the Royal Navy's William V. No--it should be the Imperial Navy. The king has always claimed that he obtained the title of Emperor in Balam. Heh, in the Imperial Navy, no matter how outstanding or excellent the average person is, he can at most become a boatswain. He cannot be an officer unless--unless you can satisfy your superior, regardless of what method is used! Only then can you be recommended to the Pritz Naval Academy as a reserve officer!

"That's how Elland was forced to leave the navy and ended up joining the White Agate, where he slowly worked his way up to captain.

"I suggest you choose a first-class cabin. That way, you'll have rooms for three to four servants, an attendant who has received etiquette lessons, an appointed chef who has excellent culinary skills, a quiet restaurant where you can enjoy the scenery, a special room for smoking cigars, and a place where you can gather and play cards..."

Hearing the guard's detailed explanation, Klein couldn't help but feel suspicious.

Noticing his expression, the guard smiled in embarrassment.

"Elland was my boss in the past. He would often treat me to drinks and ask me to help him promote the first-class cabins. But you can rest assured that everything I say is true!"

That's not the problem; money is... Klein thought silently.

Having made up his mind, he asked after some deliberation, "Sir, what advice do you have for a maritime adventurer?"

To suit Gehrman Sparrow's identity, Klein had modified his image slightly to make himself seem colder and sharper.

"Adventurer?" The guard unconsciously raised his voice.

Many people in the queue turned to look at Klein.

Guided by his spiritual intuition, Klein instinctively followed one of the gazes.

He saw a man in his thirties wearing a black top hat. He had a rugged face, weather-beaten wrinkles, a strong but not particularly tall build, and pale blue eyes that had seen much.

Another adventurer? Klein and the man looked away just as their eyes met.

At that moment, the guard forced a smile and said, "I'm sorry, I'm a little sensitive to the word adventurer. In my opinion, it's synonymous with fugitive, maritime villain, and oath-breaker. No, I'm not talking about you.

"You want sincere advice? I... Uh, you have to remember three things.

"First, do not provoke pirates. Second, do not provoke pirates. Third, do not provoke pirates!

"Unless you're a member of the navy or the Church, do not go against the pirates!"

"Eh... Don't be fooled by the island girls' enthusiasm. They're either pirates or want you to take them to Pritz or Backlund. It isn't entirely their fault. To take advantage of them, many sailors, crewmen, and passengers paint alluring pictures of the metropolis and the beautiful lives awaiting them, then kick them out of bed and abandon them right where they started."

What a bunch of vile people... In this era, people who live on the sea can't be too kind... Is the order at sea that bad? Pirates are this rampant? Klein nodded and said, "Thank you, I know what to do now."

With that, he walked over to the shortest line.

Behind him, the guard shouted, "And the legends of treasure at sea are all fake!"

After buying a second-class ticket for the White Agate, Klein returned to the hotel and waited patiently for the night to descend.

While waiting, he sampled Pritz Harbor's most famous fried fish. He thought it tasted quite good, but he definitely couldn't bear eating it all the time.

When it was close to eight o'clock, he went above the gray fog, holding the badge from Lanevus in one hand and writing the corresponding sentence in the other.

"The situation at this gathering."

Tick. Tock. Tick. Tock. When his pocket watch struck eight, he closed his eyes, leaned back in his chair, and began to recite the divination statement.

He had reason to believe that once the gathering opened, he would be able to divine something above the gray fog with the aid of this medium, which was linked to the location!

He had failed before because nothing had happened yet, but this was different. Things were happening now, and Klein had the right medium!

Soon, Klein entered a gray, blurry dream world.

He saw the Tussock River flowing quietly, a wide river valley on the two sides, and about a dozen people in different positions. They were shrouded in thin light, flickering in and out, indistinct and illusory.

One of them had black hair and green eyes. He looked quite handsome, and he was a familiar face to Klein.

Leonard Mitchell!

Chapter 491: Sherlock Moriarty in the Investigation Report

Leonard? Klein thought for a moment that he was mistaken.

However, the blur caused by the rays of light wasn't severe. Being familiar with Leonard, he was able to quickly confirm his initial judgment.

In the time it took to take a breath, Leonard disappeared, the light dispersed, and the valley returned to its midwinter silence. The scene Klein had witnessed in the dream shattered with it.

He opened his eyes and placed the badge he had obtained from Lanevus on the surface of the long bronze table.

Is it really Leonard, or a Faceless disguised as Leonard? Klein pondered the question and flipped a gold coin.

Through the medium, his spiritual intuition told him that it was Leonard Mitchell, his former teammate from Tingen's Nighthawks!

Is he the "investigator" sent by the Church of the Goddess to look into the gathering, or is he risking his life to find an opportunity to locate the target of his revenge while keeping the Nighthawks in the dark? Klein wondered with suspicion. It was difficult to reach an accurate conclusion.

Without any leads, his divination couldn't provide him with any revelations.

After a few seconds of silence, Klein gave a self-deprecating smile as he drew a crimson moon on his chest.

"I wish him the best of luck. May the Goddess watch over him."

Klein no longer dwelled on the question. He planned to learn more about the gathering before deciding whether to participate in the future or warn Leonard Mitchell anonymously.

Backlund, in a secret room in the basement of the Steam Cathedral.

Ikanser took off his hat, smoothed down his fluffy, unruly hair, and sat in the first chair on the left.

Then, he took out the ancient silver mirror, Arrodes, from a custom-made inner pocket and placed it in front of him.

To his right, opposite him, and diagonally across from him were Machinery Hivemind deacons and captains, all of whom had been summoned to the meeting by Horamick Haydn, a member of the Divine Council and Archbishop of Backlund.

The white-robed archbishop, who looked like an ordinary old man, sat calmly at the head of the table.

Seeing that everyone had arrived, he looked around and softly said, "Let's start with Ikanser. Give us an orderly account of the investigation over the past few days."

Ikanser Bernard ran a hand through his hair as he flipped through the thick dossier, and he reported concisely, "Your Grace, we were in charge of Sherlock Moriarty. After a careful investigation, and with the aid of Beyonder methods, we confirmed that he had been drawn into the affair. Prior to the incident, there was no evidence that he knew of Prince Edessak's problem.

"He and the dead Talim Dumont were friends, and he indirectly fulfilled some of the tasks entrusted to him by the prince, but there were few irregularities. At most, he had inflated some of his expense claims."

At this point, Ikanser suddenly felt uneasy because Sherlock Moriarty was also an informant for the Machinery Hivemind, and his expense claims here were likely somewhat inflated.

In any case, his work as an informant had been highly effective and outstanding, enough to offset many of his shortcomings, and he hadn't been our informant for long. Most of the money involved came from commissions... Ikanser exhaled slowly and continued his report, "Our conclusion is that he's an innocent party and that there is no hidden plot behind him. He once keenly sensed the danger at Red Rose Manor, but that is a typical example of reaching the correct conclusion through faulty reasoning. He was afraid of internal strife within the royal family, and for this reason, he had been slow in his work and hadn't conducted any substantive investigations. He had reported these concerns to us.

"Unfortunately, he still failed to avoid becoming entangled in the affair, but he was fortunate. He mentioned that the descendant of Death happened to be monitoring the vicinity of Red Rose Manor at the time; hence, he was rescued from that perilous situation when the meteorite fell. The traces at the scene amply demonstrate how terrifying that strike was. It was likely the work of 0-08."

Klein mentioned the existence of Ince Zangwill and 0-08 in his letter. As for whether or not he knew the former archbishop and the Grade 0 Sealed Artifact, no one found that suspicious, because he had spent some time with Azik Eggers. It was entirely possible that he learned the information from him. This was also the conclusion everyone reached instinctively.

As for matters regarding Grade 0 and Grade 1 Sealed Artifacts, the seven orthodox churches regularly shared general information with one another--the numbering was shared, and there was no repetition.

"However, we haven't been able to establish the full picture. There are three points that remain unconfirmed. First, 2-111 indicates that after Sherlock Moriarty fled into the forest, he didn't immediately escape far away. Instead, he remained where he was and prayed to someone. Second, it's still unknown when he and Azik Eggers got to know each other. Third, it's still unknown how he escaped the underground ruins. It's unlikely that he could've done so with his strength. In the process, he had even destroyed the descent ritual of the Aurora Order," Ikanser finally concluded.

2-111 referred to the magic mirror, Arrodes.

Horamick laughed after hearing the report and muttered, "Fraudulent expense claims..."

He then cleared his throat.

"In any case, Sherlock Moriarty is a hero of Backlund.

"If he hadn't stopped the ritual in time, and if he had been frightened at that moment and had chosen to flee, then most of us wouldn't be alive sitting here.

"Moreover, he has also displayed his faith in God and his goodwill toward us. As long as there isn't too much of a problem with him, then we can pretend that we don't know anything about his minor flaws and secrets."

"Your Grace, this is what we were thinking as well." Ikanser heaved a sigh of relief and said, "I guess his prayers in the woods, including the ritual of blowing the copper whistle, were to get in touch with Azik Eggers. But the two methods differ in speed and effect. In such a critical situation, the only thing he could do was to try to save himself, which is something that we deduced from subsequent events."

"Apart from trying to save himself, he could write his will," another Machinery Hivemind deacon quipped before he immediately reported the portion he was responsible for. "...We haven't found the underground ruins that Sherlock Moriarty described, even with the help of 2-111. We're still unable to grasp the locations of the royal family's High-Sequence Beyonders on that very day."

"It can be confirmed that the Church of Evernight received the information first. This came from a special channel of Earl Hall, but the specific situation is unknown."

"...Most of the forces of the Aurora Order in Backlund have been eliminated by us, the Nighthawks, and the Mandated Punishers. However, I suspect that they still have some hidden strength..."

"At the time of the incident, Sherlock Moriarty mentioned that Trissy was heading to Backlund, but no one has seen her ever since. According to Sherlock Moriarty, she was a key figure and was renamed Trissy Cheek."

"It cannot be determined what method the Church of Evernight used to capture the Demoness of Despair and the butler, Funkel. Divination indicates that they're still alive, but they aren't free."

One by one, the deacons and captains reported on their findings while Horamick half-closed his eyes, seemingly deep in thought.

After a few seconds of silence, he opened his eyes and slowly said, "Devote every effort to finding Trissy Cheek--if she's still alive.

"Pass me all the questions for which divination has failed to provide any effective revelations. The Church has a Saint who is good at this, although it's not necessarily more effective than 2-111.

"As for the surveillance and investigation of the royal family's High-Sequence Beyonders, that can be shelved. There's no need to worry. They've always known what we've been doing. It's also a form of warning.

"Continue the search for the underground ruins and report it to the Evernight and Storm Churches.

"Ikanser, ask 2-111 how Sherlock Moriarty escaped the ruins, as well as when he got to know Azik Eggers."

Ikanser looked at the archbishop, then at his colleagues before gritting his teeth and saying, "Yes, Your Grace."

He was dismally certain that the legends about him were about to spread beyond the few Machinery Hivemind teams under his command to every Beyonder of the Church of the God of Steam in Backlund.

Following the well-rehearsed procedure, he opened his mouth and said, "Honorable Arrodes, my question is: 'When did Sherlock Moriarty get to know Azik Eggers?'"

The silver mirror, which seemed to have eyes on both sides, suddenly began to glow with an aqueous light, quickly forming a scene:

Sherlock Moriarty stood in a room, watching a rat with a rotting belly burrow into a hole in the wall. Behind him were Old Kohler and the owner of a budget hotel.

"He got to know Azik Eggers while completing the bounty mission for him. That mission was issued by MI9, which arose from a chance conflict." Ikanser interpreted the scene.

With that, he chose to answer Arrodes's corresponding question. He held his breath as he waited for Arrodes to pose its soul-piercing question.

As expected, he saw the bright red words: "Do you know the feeling of trying your best to win someone's favor, only to be abandoned without making any progress?"

Th-this question isn't sharp enough; it's unlike Arrodes's usual style... Ikanser suddenly felt that the blood on the mirror seemed to lack its usual bloody horror. It appeared somewhat weak.

He didn't bother to think about why and immediately opened his mouth to answer, "Yes."

"Congratulations, you got it right." A new line of text appeared on the surface of the silver mirror. Its color was somewhat pale.

5th January, 9:00 a.m.

With a gray scarf around his neck, Klein arrived at Rose Wharf with his suitcase and cane.

The White Agate was docked there, looming enormous beside the people on the wharf. It was said to be capable of ferrying hundreds of passengers.

It embodied the character of the age, with chimneys, sails, and twelve cannons along its sides--necessary defenses against pirates and other vessels.

Under Captain Elland Kag's arrangement, the chosen stocky sailors and crew lined up at the foot of the gangway, some even deliberately revealing their legally owned revolvers, rifles, and knives.

This gave the passengers boarding the ship a greater sense of security. They were no longer afraid of the journey that would take them nine days to complete.

Klein stood below, looked up, and climbed the gangway above the undulating blue water.

My journey begins... He took a step forward and sighed silently.

Chapter 492: Adventurer

As soon as he stepped onto the deck and before he could enter the cabin, Klein saw a figure moving through the crowd toward him in his peripheral vision.

He looked over his shoulder with apparent indifference but concealed vigilance and saw a man in his thirties wearing a black half top hat and a trench coat of the same color.

The man had a weathered face, rugged yet strikingly masculine. His light blue eyes didn't contain a smile, as though burdened by a wealth of memories.

He looks a little familiar... Right, he's that guy I noticed at the ticket office yesterday. He seems to be an adventurer as well... He's actually wearing a trench coat at sea in January. He's quite strong... Klein casually raised his cane, pointed it diagonally downward, and said with a smile, "Good morning, we meet again."

He sounded as if he were greeting an old friend.

The rugged man, however, wasn't surprised. He stopped and nodded in a somewhat reserved manner, saying, "Cleves, a former adventurer.

"Mate, are you in the same trade?"

"I thought you knew yesterday. Gehrman Sparrow," Klein responded with a smile.

He didn't shift his cane to his left hand, because he didn't intend to shake Cleves's hand.

"I can tell." Cleves maintained two seconds of silence and said, "Being an adventurer isn't a glamorous profession. I've already changed careers and become a bodyguard. This time, I'm accompanying my employer's family to the capital of the Rorsted Archipelago."

He half-turned and pointed to another spot on the deck.

Klein followed his gesture and saw nearly ten people gathered together. They were led by a plump middle-aged gentleman with ruddy cheeks and spirited eyes. Across his double-breasted suit ran the gold chain of his pocket watch and a jeweled collar brooch.

Next to him was a lady in a wide-brimmed hat, her face completely hidden behind a veil of dark blue muslin, though her figure was shapely.

Standing in front of the couple were two children. The younger one was a boy who wasn't even ten years old. He was wearing a child's version of a tailcoat. The older one was fifteen or sixteen years old and was a lively and energetic teenage girl. Her looks couldn't be considered outstanding, but her brown eyes were bright and intelligent. Her faint freckles and puffy dress added to her playful air.

Around them were three people carrying suitcases and all sorts of items--a man and two women, all dressed as servants. One of the maids had brownish skin, clearly of Southern Continent heritage.

A man and a woman served as bodyguards for the seven. They were dressed simply and neatly in white shirts, light sweaters, black coats, dark trousers, and sturdy leather boots.

The two bodyguards made no attempt to conceal the outlines of the gun holsters at their waists. They remained vigilant as they scrutinized approaching passersby. Their gazes were sharp and their manner composed.

"Three servants and three bodyguards?" Klein asked casually.

What an extravagant entourage. Their employer must be wealthy... Klein judged instinctively.

"Yes." Cleves nodded.

Without another word, he quickly turned around and walked toward his employer's family.

Klein was stunned. He didn't know why Cleves had specifically come over to greet him.

After recalling the novels, movies, and television dramas he had seen in his previous life, he gradually understood what Cleves was implying.

He's a little wary of me, or should I say of someone who claims to be an adventurer and looks dangerous, so he introduced himself in advance to establish his identity and occupation. It's to tell me not to have any designs on his employer's family. In short, "you do what you do, I do what I do, and we stay out of each other's business"... Is this the tacit understanding between experienced adventurers and veteran bounty hunters? Interesting... Klein chuckled. He carried his suitcase and black cane as he went into the cabin. Using his ticket, he found his own room.

With a creak, he pushed open the wooden door and walked in.

The room wasn't very spacious, just large enough to fit a bed, a table, and a cupboard. There weren't even any chairs.

Its greatest advantage was its window. Sunlight from the wharf shone in, illuminating the table and bedside with pure golden spots.

A member of the crew mentioned that the washroom and bathroom were communal, with roughly eight rooms sharing each facility. If someone urgently needed one, the crew could provide a wooden chamber pot, though the passenger had to pay the cleaning fee of three pence each time... I have to be grateful that after the White Agate was renovated, numerous metal pipes were installed. The boilers provide hot water and relatively convenient living conditions. Otherwise, my journey wouldn't be pleasant... Klein silently sighed.

He quickly took out his necessities and placed them on the table for daily use.

When he was tidying up the place, he sat down on the edge of the bed which wasn't too high, and he listened to the ship's long steam whistle. He felt the power that stemmed from steam and machinery contained within it.

When the ship set sail, Klein looked out the window at the sea and gradually withdrew his thoughts. He began to think about the most important thing that would happen next--the problem of how to act as a Faceless.

While facing the True Creator's descent ritual and the powerful Mr. A, his decision to abandon escape and attempt sabotage had allowed his potion to be digested a little. Based on this feedback, he gained some new insights into the acting requirements of a Faceless.

"You can disguise yourself as anyone, but you are ultimately yourself." This is the principle Marionettist Rosago had impressed upon him... I originally imagined that "yourself" refers to my original identity, but from the looks of it, that's not the complete picture. What truly constitutes "yourself" requires serious thought... Klein leaned forward and bathed in the sunlight as though he was a thinking statue.

After a while, he came up with an idea.

Does this refer to the essence of one's identity, to one's true self deep down?

Even back on Earth, Zhou Mingrui had worn many masks to form a social persona. In a sense, much of it wasn't real.

Yes... When I become someone else, I take over their identity. To avoid discovery, I have to disguise myself socially, which is the same as wearing a different mask.

Once every mask has been removed and none remain, what kind of "yourself" does a Faceless see at the end of the day?

Is this the deeper meaning behind "yourself" in that principle?

When I face my inner thoughts, overcome my fear, and challenge the impossible free of motives imposed by society, am I really acting as myself?

This remains to be explored and verified...

At this thought, Klein changed his position, trying to sit more comfortably.

Remembering everything that had happened before, he encountered another question about acting.

In the underground ruins, I disguised myself as Ince Zangwill and successfully escaped from my predicament, but why didn't I feel any signs of digesting the potion at all?

Does this imply that to digest the Faceless potion, a superficial disguise such as that isn't enough?

Yes, this is closer to the use of Beyonder powers and not acting!

What stimulates the potion's digestion must be a disguise on a deeper level. Does it mean truly replacing someone and becoming that person on a social level? Would the disguise be proven successful only when that person's relatives and friends were unable to recognize the substitution for a long time?

In that case, the reason why I feel a sense of resonance with the Faceless potion is because I had previously disguised myself as Klein Moretti?

The first principle for a Faceless is that you can disguise yourself as anyone, but you are ultimately yourself... The second principle is to have a disguise on a deeper level that fools everyone?

But to replace a person and become that person in a societal sense is rather evil even to contemplate...

Don't tell me that I have to find people who died far from home with unfulfilled wishes?

Klein suppressed his inexplicable dread, and he drew up an initial direction for his acting attempts.

The higher the Sequence, the more difficult it is to act... He sighed, took out his pocket watch, and checked the time.

Since it was still too early for lunch and the room was too small and cramped, he decided to take a walk on the deck and enjoy the scenery in the sea breeze.

After the excitement of the first hour of the voyage, there weren't many people left on deck. Klein walked along the side of the ship, arriving at a secluded area cloaked in deep shadow.

It's a warm, sunny day... The only drawback is the strong wind that might steal my hat... He pressed the half top hat on his head and leisurely surveyed the cabin, listening to the faint sounds of music coming from inside.

Suddenly, he saw the former adventurer, Cleves, tending to something around the corner. In front of him lay a triangular blade, a dagger, and a short knife.

Cleves sensed his presence and raised his head to look at him. Maintaining the composure of a seasoned veteran, he said tersely, "They're all old companions, and they need frequent maintenance."

At this point, he added, "There are children in the cabin."

"Understood." Klein smiled in response.

Cleves lowered his head and continued working. He casually asked, "You don't seem to have these things with you?"

"I'm used to using weapons that keep pace with the times," Klein said vaguely. "And I maintain them frequently."

Cleves silently raised his dagger, looked at it against the sunlight, and said to himself, "At sea, guns alone aren't enough.

"Pirates will board ships, and there will be a lot of people. After you empty your gun, you won't have the opportunity to reload again. Although these guys no longer keep pace with the times, they're still useful."

Very professional... As expected of a former adventurer... Klein leaned against the side of the boat and said half-jokingly, "If there really are pirates boarding the ship, I'm more likely to choose not to fight back."

Cleves turned his head and stared at him for a good three seconds before retracting his gaze. Then, as he packed up his things, he said in a low voice, "It seems that you don't need me to remind you. You've already understood the rules of the sea.

"Bounty hunters who roam the land are often unable to be adventurers at sea."

He skillfully hid the dagger, short knife, and triangular blade under his clothes, with a dexterity that dazzled Klein.

"Thank you." Klein smiled and nodded.

Without speaking any further, Cleves turned around and returned to the cabin, leaving behind only his broad, sturdy back.

Klein smirked, shook his head, and turned his gaze to the side of the ship.

Blue waves gently rose and fell, and silvery-white flying fish occasionally leaped out of the water and soared through the air.

These fish can "fly" and swim. Fishermen and sailors consider them the family of the Lord of Storms, so even when caught in nets, they're returned to the sea... Klein leisurely admired the sunlit sea and the flying fish above it. An irresistible thought flashed through his mind.

Hmm, I wonder what their meat tastes like...

Chapter 493: Hunting

"Honorable Arrodes, my second question is: 'How did Sherlock Moriarty escape from the ruins?'" Ikanser felt far more relaxed than before.

The surface of the silver mirror shimmered, rapidly forming an image of Sherlock Moriarty with his back against the wall and his fists clenched tight.

Then, Ikanser Bernard and the surrounding Machinery Hivemind deacons and captains saw the private detective flash an exaggerated smile, turn around, and dash out with his gun drawn.

At this moment, caught up in the scene, they all felt an inexplicable sense of sadness and excitement.

The image in the mirror jumped, revealing Sherlock Moriarty wielding his revolver as he shot at the altar to no avail. The scene of those disintegrating bullets caused everyone present to be uneasy.

Then, Sherlock Moriarty threw out a brass key, and the altar showed signs of instability due to corruption.

Amid the erupting blast of air, Mr. A fell to the ground and Sherlock Moriarty fled the temple in a sorry state.

At this point, the image changed, revealing the slightly turbid Tussock River. Sherlock Moriarty and Mr. A floated in the water, looking up into the sky where there were no clouds or fog.

Almost instantly, Mr. A became transparent and disappeared, leaving only Sherlock Moriarty looking around in astonishment.

"The Church of Evernight's reinforcements?" Ikanser said with a frown. "Unfortunately, he didn't mention what appeared in the sky in his letter. We have no way of guessing. Was he trying to sell this secret for a good price, or was he simply affected by it and lost the relevant memories? Also, his experience of escaping from the underground ruins reveals nothing. It seems like the corresponding clues have been concealed at the same time..."

He swiftly delivered his analysis in methodical sentences, and then without too great a psychological burden, he chose to answer Arrodes's question in return instead of taking a risk.

Arrodes doesn't seem to be in the mood to be a prankster today. I can make the most of it... As he consoled himself, Ikanser saw bloody words appear on the surface of the mirror.

His heart skipped a beat. He had a bad premonition, suspecting that Arrodes had already returned to his usual "state."

The bloodlike words writhed and quickly formed into a question:

"Who was the person whose favor you gave your all to win, only to end up being abandoned?"

His head buzzed, and all color drained from Ikanser's face before flushing red.

The question pierced a deep wound and left him at a loss.

If I name him, his reputation will be ruined before the night is out... I've already become a kind of "legend" in some sense... Ikanser swallowed with great difficulty and bitterly said, "I choose punishment."

A bolt of lightning immediately descended. However, it was different from before. It was no longer silvery-white and was tinged faintly green.

It hit him right on the head, causing his hair to stand on end and flicker with electric light.

He shook as violently as dice in a cup, as though he had been dosed with a hallucinogen.

Archbishop Horamick sighed, closed his eyes, and muttered to himself, "A Grade 0 Sealed Artifact?"

When Ikanser recovered, he looked around and said, "There's one more question. Where did the key that Sherlock Moriarty used to destroy the descent ritual come from?

"Which one of you is going to use 2-111?"

All the Machinery Hivemind deacons and captains looked at each other. For a moment, no one responded.

The sound of water splashing against the ship's hull seemed to be the only sound left in the world. The sea at night was both noisy and quiet.

Klein suddenly awoke and opened his eyes. He saw the wooden ceiling bathed in the crimson moon's veil.

His spiritual intuition told him that something was happening outside.

Is someone having a rendezvous? He tilted his head to listen, vaguely able to hear some unnatural sounds.

He sat up, put on his gloves, and put on his coat.

His eyes turned dark as he took out a gold coin, flipped it, and quickly performed a divination.

After receiving no revelations of danger, he took out the revolver under his pillow and put it in his pocket.

Once suitably prepared, Klein opened the door and walked out of the room, following the sound to the upper deck.

At this moment, far out at sea, away from industrial pollution, the crimson moon quietly hung there, mysterious and dreamy.

After carefully bypassing a few patrolling crew members, Klein arrived at the area where the disturbance originated. He could smell the faint scent of blood.

With the help of the moonlight, he looked over and saw the former adventurer, Cleves, crouching by the side of the ship and arranging something.

There were three people hiding in the shadows of the cabin about a dozen meters away from this gentleman. One of them was Cleves's companion, the female bodyguard in the black coat, and the remaining two were the children of their employer, a girl of fourteen or fifteen and a young gentleman no older than ten.

The two youngsters wore thick cotton nightgowns and outer coats. It was obvious that they had come out in a hurry.

They trembled amidst the chilling night wind, but they still crouched there brimming with energy, looking at Cleves with their bright eyes.

Playing hide and seek? Klein joked inwardly.

He deliberately made his footsteps louder, causing Cleves and the others to turn their gazes toward him.

"Friend, what happened?" Klein adopted the manner of certain bounty hunters he had known in East Borough.

But he still maintained Gehrman Sparrow's distinctive cold, incisive demeanor.

Cleves replied, unperturbed, "A private job, an unexpected hunt, but one worth anticipating."

A hunt? Klein's interest was suddenly piqued.

The reason he had named himself Gehrman was that it was the name of the first hunter in a game he had played in his previous life, and it suited his idea of hunting evil at sea.

Klein was in no hurry to inquire about the reason. With the hand that wore Creeping Hunger, he pointed toward the nearby shadows. "Private job? Doing a private job in front of your employer?"

Cleves, who was squatting there, glanced at the boy and the girl and said, without changing his tone, "Cecile wasn't careful enough and ended up waking Donna and Denton up. She had no choice but to let them follow."

The girl called Donna wrinkled her nose when she heard her name mentioned. She looked up curiously and asked Klein, "Uncle, are you also an adventurer?"

Uncle? Even counting my age on Earth, I'm at most ten years older than you! Klein said in amusement, "No, you can't use the word 'also.' Strictly speaking, I'm the only adventurer here; they're just bodyguards right now."

He turned to Cleves and said, "Hey, mate, what prey did you discover?"

Cleves looked into the faint crimson waters and said, "A murloc."

Murloc? That's a Beyonder creature! Even though it's among the lowest level, it's still quite difficult for ordinary people to handle. They would need at least five or six people and four or five spears to have a chance... That's right, the scales on a murloc's body are very hard. Revolvers can inflict only minor damage. They would need rifles... Klein raised his eyebrows and asked, "What do you plan on doing? And how are you sure it's a murloc?"

Cleves pointed to the edge of the ship and said, "There are traces of its mucus here. One to two hours ago, it tried to climb onto the ship to attack the passengers, but the deck was still bustling with activity and there were lots of sailors and crew."

Klein took a few steps forward and saw that there were some traces of green corrosion on the side of the ship.

He recalled the information he had encountered in Tingen, which agreed with the books. He asked with great interest, "Why does it have to be alone rather than part of a group?"

He remembered that murlocs tended to live in groups.

"If it's a group, they'll simply destroy the ship's hull and let everyone sink. Moreover, the area around this channel and the surrounding sea have already been cleared of murlocs. The Church of Storms really enjoys hunting them," Cleves solemnly explained.

That's because murlocs are probably one of the main ingredients of the Sequence 9 Sailor potion... Klein stroked the revolver in his pocket and asked with a smile, "Are you confident?"

Cleves didn't directly answer him and instead opened a paper bag beside him. Inside the bag were some pig organs still stained with blood. This was the source of the blood Klein had smelled.

"All murlocs like this kind of food and are unable to resist its allure. Of course, these monsters love human organs the most, so in many sea legends, they recommend preparing some pig or beef organs from the ship's kitchen or canned organs," Cleves said as he sprinkled some granules. "Pepper granules can cause the murlocs to experience a marijuana-like exhilaration and lose some of their sense of balance. This can last for about a minute, and after that, the murlocs will be exhausted after the heightened excitement subsides."

He then took out a wooden box from his clothes and smeared the dark green paste over the tip of the triangular blade, dagger, and short knife.

"The mint cream that's popular in Pritz Harbor is a unique sweetener for humans, but to murlocs, it's a deadly blood toxin.

"In addition, I borrowed two rifles from the sailors. I secured an agreement that this area would remain undisturbed for twenty minutes, and I spent a sizable sum of money. However, as long as I can successfully kill a murloc, then I'll be able to earn ten, twenty, or even thirty times my costs."

As expected of an experienced adventurer, he's exceptionally knowledgeable about his prey's weaknesses and habits... Listening to him speak, I feel like they have a chance of successfully hunting the murloc, even if they aren't Beyonders... In the face of traps and firearms, Low-Sequence Beyonders really aren't much stronger than the average person... It's not like there are no Low-Sequence Beyonders who die in gang wars... However, murlocs are creatures that seem to wear full-body armor. It's not easy to kill them. It might be injured, but it could still escape... Curious, Klein asked, "You seem to have killed quite a number of murlocs?"

"Understanding the characteristics of common sea monsters is a prerequisite for an adventurer's survival." Cleves didn't react to the praise as he remained calm and silent.

As they conversed, the girl Donna and the boy Denton squatted in the shadows and listened with relish. They found all of this to be the most interesting thing in the world.

Yes, I also need to brush up on my studies in this area... Klein smiled and said, "So that's how it is. I didn't disturb you, did I?"

Cleves skewered a portion of the organs on a rod and said in a deep voice, "If you want to participate, take care of Donna and Denton so that Cecile won't be distracted."

"Sure." Klein, who wanted to watch from the sidelines, smiled and agreed.

Chapter 494: A Bite of a Murloc

The red moon hung high in the sky as Klein walked over to Donna and Denton and squatted down beside them.

Cecile, Cleves's female companion, let out a sigh of relief. She picked up the rifle on the deck, bent down, and quickly moved in another direction. She put about ten meters between herself and the peppered pig organs.

"Uncle, is it starting...?" The mischievous, freckled teenage girl, Donna, suddenly felt a little nervous. However, her face was filled with curiosity and anticipation.

Klein raised his left index finger to his mouth, motioning for the two youngsters to be quiet.

At times like this, he couldn't help but thank Roselle. It was because of the efforts of this senior transmigrator that some of his habitual gestures had become common body language in the Northern Continent, without causing misunderstandings.

It was said that in the early part of the Fifth Epoch, this "don't talk" gesture was an insult in Loen. But in certain parts of the Southern Continent, it means "kiss me"... Klein's thoughts wandered briefly.

Donna and Denton didn't dare to say anything else. They just squatted there quietly, watching with rapt attention as Cleves prepared for the battle.

The former adventurer picked up a rod and cast the line, with some pig organs hanging from it, over the side of the ship.

With a splash, the bait entered the water.

After calmly scattering the remaining organs, Cleves collected his weapons and retreated, step by step, to hide in the shadows opposite Cecile. The two of them formed an angle of roughly sixty degrees with the spot where the rod rested against the ship's side.

After propping his triangular blade and other weapons nearby, he raised his rifle and settled into his aim.

The deck had gone completely quiet, leaving only the thrum of the steam engine and the crash of waves against the ship.

As the minutes ticked by, Donna and Denton couldn't help but switch from squatting to sitting, with their backs against the planks of the cabin, trying to ease the numbness in their legs.

At that moment, they saw the rod on the side of the boat sink a little.

A muffled scraping sound rapidly grew closer and closer. Suddenly, a figure jumped onto the deck.

It was a monster bathed in crimson moonlight. Its entire body was covered in dark green scales, and green slime flowed over it.

It didn't have much in common with humans. It was like a giant fish that had grown four strong limbs, and there was conspicuous webbing between its digits.

The murloc was more than 1.9 meters tall, with round eyes and gills on its cheeks. It resembled a demon from legend, making Donna cover her mouth to prevent herself from screaming.

At the same time, she covered the mouth of her brother, Denton, as well.

Good thinking... Klein smiled to himself as he carefully examined the murloc.

Unlike the Sailor Rampager he had seen before, real murlocs didn't have human-like brains. They were pure monsters.

The murloc vigilantly scanned the surroundings before squatting down. It picked up the scattered pig organs, quickly stuffed them into its mouth, and chewed audibly.

The light in its mostly white eyes gradually faded, as if it had fallen into a dream.

It isn't very intelligent... Klein shook his head and made his judgment.

Bang!

Cleves pulled the trigger, and a bullet flew from the rifle. It instantly struck the murloc's chest, causing its scales to shatter and blood to splatter.

"Wa!" The murloc let out a childlike cry, and it threw itself at Cleves, who was hidden in the shadows. It was as fast as a steam train.

At this moment, Cecile, who was in another position, opened fire as well.

Bang!

The bullet from the rifle struck the murloc's ribs, causing many scales to scatter and the tall figure to stagger.

The murloc, having eaten the pepper granules, became visibly sluggish. It stopped, unsure of which enemy to attack first.

This gave Cleves and Cecile a chance to calmly reload.

They took aim again and pulled their triggers.

Bang! Bang!

Blooms of blood erupted in succession, and the pain restored clarity to the murloc's eyes.

It flipped and pounced, dodging the follow-up shots, and closed in on Cleves as though uninjured.

Cleves calmly set down the rifle in his hand and picked up the triangular blade that was leaning to his side.

Instead of dodging, he leaped forward and rolled to the side of the murloc. The triangular blade in his hand ruthlessly and accurately pierced the patch on his prey's side where the scales had shattered.

The murloc spun around abruptly, stirring up a gust of wind. It violently flung away both the triangular blade and Cleves, causing the former adventurer to crash onto the deck.

The murloc shook its head, as though overcome by intense discomfort. It didn't attack Cleves and Cecile again, but instead took large strides to the side of the ship in an attempt to jump into the sea.

Bang!

Cecile's bullet hit it again, raising another bloom of blood, but still failing to stop it.

With two steps, the murloc reached a suitable spot. It bent its knees and prepared to jump up.

However, its body was too weak to exert its full strength. Its leap clearly fell short, so it could only land back on the deck.

Bang!

The murloc withstood the shot and tried to flip over the side of the ship.

Seeing that it was about to escape, Klein took out his revolver.

Just then, a loud bang came from another direction!

The murloc's left eye turned into a bloody hole, and one could vaguely see a pale gelatinous substance squirming inside.

It wasn't dead yet. It lay on the deck, trying its best to crawl and get up again.

After a few seconds, the poison took effect and it twitched and died.

Klein followed the sound and saw a middle-aged man step out of the shadows on the other side of the cabin.

The middle-aged man was wearing a thick dark red coat, white slacks, and the ship-shaped hat standard to the era.

He was holding an iron-colored musket that was a relic of an earlier age as white smoke billowed out of its thick black muzzle.

Klein had heard an attendant introduce the man before and recognized him. He was the captain of the White Agate, Elland Kag.

With obvious wrinkles around his eyes, across his forehead, and at the corners of his mouth, Elland walked toward Cleves and said with a smile, "As a captain, I must ensure that no accidents happen.

"Forgive me for watching from the sidelines all this time."

Cleves had already stood up. He showed no emotion.

"This is your ship.

"According to convention, you have the right to share in the spoils."

Elland turned his head to look at Klein and the others and said with a smile, "The next resupply of water and food is in two days' time. You'll have to think of a way to preserve the murloc's corpse.

"How about this? Sell it to me at a cheaper price; the difference is the reward I deserve."

"This is the best solution." Cleves and Cecile exchanged glances and agreed to Elland's request. "For 130 pounds, it's all yours."

The murloc Beyonder ingredient has a market price of 150 to 200 pounds. Considering the other spiritual parts, 130 pounds is indeed cheap... However, this is Cleves and Cecile's only practical option. This is Elland's ship, and he has a large group of armed sailors and crew to help him. If the negotiations break down, they could send everyone here to the bottom within minutes... Of course, that assumes I don't intervene... Yes, it's evident that Cleves and Cecile aren't Beyonders, at least not Beyonders of the combat and shooting domains. As for Elland, I'm not so sure... Klein stood up and listened to the deal.

"No, you seem to have misunderstood something. I'm not threatening you. One hundred and fifty pounds. That's a fair price." Elland Kag called for a sailor and gave him the key to the safe.

"Are you the 'Just Elland'?" Only then did Cecile seem to recall his epithet at sea.

Elland laughed and said, "Yes."

At this moment, Donna and Denton, stunned by the intense battle and the living monster, jumped to their feet and ran over to the murloc both excited and afraid.

"Is... is it really dead?" Donna kicked the murloc's body with her toe, then jumped away and hid behind her younger brother as though afraid it might revive.

"It really is a monster!" Denton took a deep breath and opened his eyes wide.

"There are many monsters at sea. Apart from having four limbs and being able to stand, murlocs bear no other resemblance to humans." Elland smiled gently.

He squatted down and took out a knife, slicing the cheek beneath the murloc's eye, revealing white, tender skin that was tainted with some red.

"The most delicious part of a murloc's body is best eaten raw." Elland carefully sliced a piece of flesh and handed it to Donna. "You remind me of my daughter. Unfortunately, she's grown up and has a family of her own."

"I-I'm afraid to eat it..." Donna said, looking at the thin sliver of flesh held at the tip of the knife.

"Haha, which one of you wants to try?" Elland laughed and looked around.

After confirming that there were no warnings from his spiritual intuition, Klein nodded.

"I'm very curious."

Elland immediately handed him the knife.

"Give it a try. On land, even nobles might not have a chance to eat it.

"These murlocs are fish monsters. You can think of them as a mutated species of fish."

He was trying to dispel the siblings' fears.

Klein wanted to ask if there was any wasabi, soy sauce, or any other condiments, but seeing that the other party didn't mention it, he was afraid to appear ignorant.

He took the knife, bit into the bloody flesh, and swallowed it.

The flesh melted in his mouth at once. The taste of blood was very faint, lending it a pleasant saltiness. It perfectly highlighted the meat's fresh sweetness.
