Klein chewed twice, savoring the fish's exquisite tenderness and freshness. It was the first time in his life that he had experienced something like that.

"Excellent." He wasn't stingy with his praise as he gave a thumbs up.

Donna watched the whole thing with curiosity, suddenly interested in the flesh on the murloc's cheek.

Curiosity overcame her fear and disgust, and she asked to try some.

Elland obliged her and smiled as he watched her squeeze her eyes shut, her face scrunching into a grimace as she bit into the meat.

Donna's expression gradually relaxed, and she quickly opened her eyes. She excitedly praised, "An indescribable delicacy!"

Her example prompted Denton, Cecile, and the others to share what little cheek meat remained. It satisfied their palates while leaving them wanting more; the flavor delighted them, but the quantity did not.

Seeing that Elland had eaten the last piece, Cleves pointed at the murloc's body and said, "The meat around the ribs is suitable for frying, while the belly meat should be roasted. The other parts taste bad."

"I share your thoughts," Elland said with a chuckle. "I'll have the chef prepare it immediately. On such a night, we should enjoy good food and wine together and exchange legends of the sea. What could be more enjoyable?"

I'm looking forward to it... However, why did a perfectly fine hunt turn into a culinary exchange... Klein swallowed.

Chapter 495: Treasure Legends

An extra layer of asbestos had been placed beneath the grill on deck to prevent the coal sparks from being blown out by the wind and scorching the surface of the ship.

The fat cook, wearing an apron and a tall white hat invented by Roselle, used a fine brush to coat the long, white strips of fish with a secret seasoning of basil, fennel, sea salt, pepper, and lemon juice, frequently flipping them over to ensure that both sides were evenly heated.

The flesh of the murloc's belly was fatty, dripping oil and causing the coal beneath the iron mesh to sizzle and flare up.

An enticing aroma began to fill the air, and Klein inhaled again and again.

In front of him were a round table and a chair. On the table stood a unique-looking bottle of wine. The wine was golden-red and looked slightly viscous when swirled.

"Sonia blood wine is brewed from the sugar-wood juice from the region surrounding Golden Spring. It tastes like diluted honey mixed with blood. It's sweet and smooth, but it's deceptively intoxicating. If you fancy a lady and want to treat her to a drink, you can consider this. It will make her drink more without realizing it. Haha, that's under the premise that you're able to hold your liquor as well as she can." Noticing Klein's scrutiny, Captain Elland Kag explained half-jokingly.

As expected of a former boatswain, he's remarkably adept at self-deprecation... Klein sat down and maintained his aloof demeanor as he remarked, "A qualified adventurer wouldn't indulge in alcohol."

Cleves, who was beside him, nodded.

"Only when they return home will adventurers allow themselves to drink."

"What a pity." Elland turned to look at Donna and Denton who were staring curiously at the blood wine. He laughed and said, "This isn't something that minors should experience."

"I've had it before. It tastes really good!" Donna retorted immediately. "It's just... It's just that I fell asleep without realizing it and slept an entire afternoon..."

"I remember that you drank two cups that time!" the little gentleman, Denton, said enviously.

Elland refused to indulge them and instead ordered a cup of sweet iced tea for each of the siblings and casually explained, "There are a lot of Southerners on this ship."

Denton dragged his disappointed gaze away and looked at the murloc which had been stripped of its valuable materials. He said rather innocently, "Actually... Actually, it's not that scary. It's just a bigger, uglier fish with four limbs!"

Donna shot her younger brother a glance.

"Congratulations, you've finally recognized the essence of it."

She then looked eagerly at Cleves and Elland.

"Uncle, Uncle, are there many of these monsters in the sea?"

Murlocs didn't possess unimaginable Beyonder powers. In the eyes of ordinary people, they were akin to ferocious beasts at sea or living incarnations of legendary monsters.

Elland chuckled and said, "No, along the main sea route and in the surrounding waters, there are very few monsters like this. They've been cleared out a long time ago. You have to be quite lucky to encounter a murloc.

"Think about it, if I can often kill monsters worth 200 pounds or more, then I certainly wouldn't be the captain of a passenger ship. I'd organize my own hunting boat to hunt down those walking gold pounds!"

That makes a lot of sense! Klein secretly cheered him on.

According to his observation, the Beyonder ingredient from a murloc was its bladder. That azure aqueous light looked like a gemstone.

Cleves raised the black tea which a sailor had just delivered. He first smelled it before taking a sip.

"Only by leaving the main sea route and going deep into the ocean, into waters often shrouded by fog or storms, will there be a good chance of encountering such monsters, but that would be very dangerous.

"Apart from scaly murlocs who can climb, there are also legends at sea of Nagas with human upper bodies and serpentine lower halves. They have six arms and are very agile."

Elland continued on the topic.

"There are also giant octopuses that can spit corrosive fluids, terrifying sea monsters that can capsize a ship with a casual blow, mermaids with intoxicating songs that make one unwilling to leave, blue giant dragons that can produce lightning, and giant birds whose wings can summon hurricanes. Heh heh, I've never seen any of these before. They're all legends of the sea, and no one knows whether they're real or fake."

Mermaids... Klein's expression didn't change.

"Very interesting," Donna exclaimed, fascinated, longing evident in her eyes.

Denton looked around, and seeing that Klein hadn't spoken, he curiously asked, "Uncle, you're an adventurer too. Have you seen such monsters before?"

Klein was stunned for a moment and then gave a faint smile.

"Once. Back then, there were five of us, and we chanced upon a... a single murloc. After an intense fight, we finally killed it."

This was a real experience of his in Tingen City; it was also his first encounter with an official Beyonder who had lost control of himself.

At that time, he and Old Neil had helped to eliminate a mutated Sailor at the request of Swain, the old owner of the Evil Dragon Bar.

Thinking of this, Klein felt nostalgic and wistful. His cold, sharp expression softened noticeably.

"Five people?" While asking, Donna secretly counted the number of people who had been involved in the battle.

1, 2, 3... She discovered that only three people had taken action to kill the murloc.

Without waiting for Klein's response, Elland said with a hint of surprise, "A chance encounter?"

"Yes," Klein answered frankly.

"Were there any casualties?" Elland pressed.

Klein shook his head.

"A few minor injuries."

"A chance encounter and only five of you managed to kill a murloc... All of you are very powerful." Cleves gave his own evaluation.

His companion, Cecile, nodded to express her astonishment.

The battle just now had been brief, and the murloc had seemed vulnerable, but she and Cleves both knew very well that without the prepared bait, the pepper granules' hallucinatory effects and subsequent fatigue, the mint cream's deadly effect on murlocs, and the two borrowed rifles, it was impossible to imagine how many people would have died to kill it.

"Indeed, very powerful." Elland looked thoughtfully at Klein.

It was a team composed of four Sequence 9s and one Sequence 8... Klein said with a half-smile and a sigh, "At that time, I was still very young, and I didn't even have any combat experience. I only provided support."

"Uncle, you're still very young!" Donna nodded her head vigorously.

I like the sound of that... Klein keenly noticed that Captain Elland had relaxed considerably after hearing that.

At this moment, several crew members had brought over a large ceramic plate. On top of it were pieces of meat that had been fried to a golden hue. Green basil leaves added splashes of color, and a captivating fragrance assaulted their noses.

Elland raised his glass of Sonia blood wine and offered a toast.

"To a beautiful night. May the Storm be with us!"

"To a beautiful night!" Donna and Denton cheered and sipped their sweet iced tea.

Klein chose to clink glasses with a cup of black tea.

He forked a piece of meat from the murloc's ribs, finding it firm and lean. However, the vegetable oil it had absorbed remedied its dryness. As he chewed, it yielded a pleasantly chewy texture and rich fragrance.

It's certainly inferior to the cheek meat, but it's still quite good. It's better than all the fish I've eaten in Backlund and Pritz Harbor... Klein thought with satisfaction.

Elland put down the knife and fork in his hands, took a sip of Sonia blood wine, and returned to the earlier topic with a sigh.

"At sea, the greatest danger doesn't come from monsters, but from pirates.

"They sail wherever they please. No one can defend against them in advance."

"Uncle Captain, will we encounter pirates?" Denton asked with concern as he swallowed the fried fish.

Elland laughed.

"The route to the Rorsted Archipelago is the safest in the world, with colonial islands en route every two to three days, allowing us to dock. The route is also patrolled by ships from the Imperial Navy and the Church of Storms.

"Even if pirates come wandering here, they wouldn't do anything too excessive. After seeing our cannons, they would at most extort a payment."

Seeing that the two minors had calmed down, Elland added, "But from the south of the Rorsted Archipelago, or as we continue east, we'll have to rely on the Lord's blessing.

"Many pirates are active in these areas, playing hide-and-seek with the ships of the navy and the Church. If we're lucky, we'll be able to reach the Southern Continent and every destination smoothly. But if we aren't lucky, we'll encounter some relatively powerful pirates, including the fleets of the Seven Pirate Admirals and even the Four Kings.

"However, you don't have to be too worried. Ever since Nast became the King of the Five Seas and enacted the rules of sea passage, most pirates will only rob you of all your wealth without doing anything too extreme.

"Around here, we're most afraid of encountering the captain of the Black Tulip, Admiral Hell Ludwell. He'll get his subordinates to kill everyone on board, throwing the innocents into hell. Next would be Admiral of Blood Senor. He loves blood, and he allows his subordinates to partake in heinous acts. Many girls have suffered under them before being sold to different islands..."

Donna shuddered as she subconsciously changed the topic.

"I heard that there are a lot of treasures at sea!"

"There are legends of treasures, but most of them are fake." Elland glanced at Cleves and said, "Six stories are the most famous. Ranked first is the Death's Key. Rumor has it that at the end of the Fourth Epoch, Death, who had caused the Pale Disaster, was attacked by the seven gods and ended up perishing. 'He' attempted to return to the Southern Continent and created raging storms and waves, an impassable barrier. It completely obstructed the sea route between the Northern and Southern Continents. This is the legend behind the origin of the Berserk Sea. However, 'He' eventually failed to return to the Southern Continent and ended up vanishing in the sea."

When he said this, Elland sighed wistfully.

"It's said that in some secret place in the Berserk Sea, the treasure left behind by Death is waiting for a person bearing the proper key to find and open it, but no one knows what the key looks like, or where it will appear.

"Ranked second is the Fountain of Unaging. It's deep in the Sonia Sea. Rumor has it that one of the Four Kings, the King of Immortality Agalito, drank the waters of the Fountain of Unaging."

Chapter 496: The Sea Holds Promise

"Fountain of Unaging? Will you really gain eternal youth if you drink it?" Donna widened her eyes and asked curiously.

She felt no anticipation; she was still young.

Elland didn't answer at once. Instead, he forked a piece of fried murloc rib meat, ate it in two bites, and took a sip of Sonia wine.

The sweet, smooth taste completely offset the greasy richness of the fried food... His eyes were half-closed, and he looked every inch the gourmet.

After pondering for a few seconds, he slowly answered Donna's question, "I don't know if there's a Fountain of Unaging, nor am I certain whether the King of Immortality Agalito drank its waters. I know only one thing: when I was very young, I heard the legend of this pirate king. Like the King of the Five Seas Nast, he seems to live forever."

"Their beards must be very long, hanging past their chests!" Denton gave his opinion.

"In fact, the King of the Five Seas, Nast, has a beard that only reaches below his neck. He sits on the deck, draped in a splendid black robe trimmed in silver. He wears a crown twice as tall as his head and overlooks everything like a deity..." Elland's tone gradually lowered as though his memories had sunk into a mire from which he could not escape.

"Uncle Captain, have you ever met the King of the Five Seas?" Donna asked excitedly.

This was the most legendary pirate. His name resounded across the seas, and even the children of the port cities knew his name.

Generations of people had grown up listening to stories about him!

In a sense, many people regarded Nast as the true pirate king... I remember one condition for advancing as a Black Emperor is to equate one's name with "emperor," entrenching it deep in the hearts of the people... Is this an early or simplified version of an attempt? I wonder what Sequence the King of the Five Seas is at now... Although Klein was focused on sampling the murloc meat, his mind whirled as a result of the group's discussion.

Faced with Donna's question, Elland said with a sigh, "At that time, I was still very young and serving on the William V. Once, our fleet tried to cross the Strait of Calamity in the Berserk Sea and encountered the Black Emperor's ship there.

"In those few minutes, everyone, including the fleet captain, lost the will to fight. Fortunately, Nast didn't give the order to attack us."

"Very cool!" the boy Denton cheered, his eyes shining.

Elland didn't continue the topic. He smiled and said, "As for the King of Immortality Agalito, I've never met him in person. I only know that his bounty notice depicts a pale middle-aged man. How pale? Let me give an example you can understand. It's as though he's been dead for some time, a corpse just beginning to rot."

When Donna and Denton heard this analogy, they subconsciously looked at the badly mutilated murloc's corpse, and their throats bobbed involuntarily.

"Of course, the most important thing in a bounty notice isn't the appearance. It's the bounty itself. In Loen alone, Agalito is worth 100,000 pounds, and his bounty is the lowest among the Four Kings." Elland changed the subject and said, "Let's continue with the treasure stories. Ranked third is the legacy of the Solomon Empire. In the Fourth Epoch, when that vast empire collapsed, the royal family loaded treasures that would make even the gods jealous aboard a ship. They steered it deep into the Fog Sea, awaiting an opportunity to rebuild their dynasty. However, five hundred years passed, then a thousand, then fifteen hundred, yet it never reappeared."

"Rumor has it that the King of the Five Seas, Nast, inherited part of the Solomon Empire's legacy. No one knows if he's a descendant of the real Black Emperor," Cecile added with interest.

"Fog Sea? Western shore of the Northern Continent?" Donna recalled her geography lessons.

"Right," Cleves responded simply.

West of the Northern Continent was the Fog Sea; to its east was the Sonia Sea, to its south was the Berserk Sea, and to its north was the North Sea. The east and west sides of the Southern Continent were similar to those of the Northern Continent, while to its south was the Polar Sea. Together, they formed the Five Seas.

The Loen Kingdom was backed by the Hornacis mountain range and the inland Midseashire. Its eastern coast was the Sonia Sea, and it stretched south to Desi Bay. It occupied several of the best entrances to the Berserk Sea, but it had no access to the Fog Sea.

"Is that so..." Donna didn't have much interest in the faraway treasure, so she shifted her attention and asked, "What about the fourth treasure?"

"It belongs to the last empire of the Fourth Epoch, Trunsoest. Rumor has it that they built a huge ship the size of a city, and they transferred all their treasures onto it. Curiously, their escape proved futile. Although the designated passengers and crew all reached the port on time, none of them survived.

"However, that ship vanished on its own. To this day, people often claim to see a gigantic ship sail silently past them on foggy nights. It's called the Specter Empire, and it appears throughout the Sonia Sea. Heh, at least, that's how all the stories go." Elland looked up at the red moon hanging high in the sky and spoke in a mocking tone that did nothing to conceal his longing.

Perhaps we will see it pass us by tomorrow night--no, tonight! Donna thought with anticipation and excitement.

Klein finished the rest of his food, drank his black tea, and listened with interest to what Elland had to say about the rest of the treasures.

"The fifth is the Lost City of Newins. Rumor has it that at the bottom of the Fog Sea, there lies a civilization of intelligent creatures. Around that patch of sea, seafarers and adventurers often find special items which all point to ancient Newins. However, the members of this civilization have yet to appear. It is as though it has been lost to the world." Elland finished his Sonia blood wine and said, "That is an inheritance left behind by a civilization. The magnitude of its wealth is absolutely unimaginable."

After pausing for two seconds, Elland put down his glass and laughed.

"In fact, the treasure I look forward to most is one supported by more accounts than mere legend: the Sunken Laurel. More than a century ago, it carried gold, jewelry, and various valuables that the kingdom had obtained from East Balam. After straying from the sea route, it sank in an unknown area somewhere between the Berserk Sea and the Sonia Sea. It hasn't been found to this day.

"It's said that the items aboard are worth millions of pounds!"

"Millions of pounds?" Donna blurted the number in astonishment.

As a merchant's daughter who had been educated for many years, she had a vague understanding of what that sum represented.

In Loen, a millionaire was a genuinely rich person, second only to the top noble families and tycoons!

Millions of pounds? As a member of the National Atmospheric Pollution Council, a major shareholder in the Coim Company, Mrs. Mary, who allowed me to join the Quelaag Club for free, is only worth a few hundred thousand pounds, and even so, she's rich enough even among aristocrats and merchants. She's a popular divorced lady, and there are even children of aristocrats who have proposed to her... Klein quickly found a suitable comparison.

Elland sighed with a smile.

"If I can find the Sunken Laurel, then I won't have to be a captain anymore. I'll head to Backlund and become a philanthropist. I'll purchase land, donate to political parties, and obtain a hereditary aristocratic title!"

I've heard Talim mention that a baronetcy costs about 300,000 pounds, and a barony about 800,000 pounds... If you manage to get your hands on the treasure, the titles of viscount and even earl are within the realm of possibility... Millions of pounds! Klein helped Elland perfect his plan.

"If it were me, I wouldn't do that. I'd buy a huge manor." Cecile also began to imagine her life after finding the treasure. "I want to hire many servants and helpers, plant large tracts of wheat, build vineyards, and make wine for myself... I also want a room where I can sunbathe, cows, sheep, and horses grazing leisurely, and bread from my own mill, like a beautiful pastoral painting..."

Elland laughed when he heard that.

"Ma'am, do you know how much a manor like this is worth?"

"No, I don't." Cecile shook her head.

"It's only a few thousand pounds. If you find the Sunken Laurel, you can buy a thousand of those manors!" Elland used exact numbers to illustrate the value of the treasure.

A thousand manors? Cecile couldn't help but raise her cup and drink a mouthful of black tea.

Before this, she had known that several million pounds was a lot of money, but she hadn't expected the sum to be this vast!

In order to alleviate the shock in her heart, she looked at Cleves and said, "Boss, if you find the Sunken Laurel, what do you plan to buy? No, what sort of life do you wish to have?"

Cleves fell silent for a moment before saying, "Return home. I'll hug my wife and children and tell them that I no longer need to go adventuring at sea."

Not a bad guy... Klein nodded slightly.

Donna looked at him curiously.

"Uncle Adventurer, what about you?"

Klein replied expressionlessly, "I'd tell myself to stop sleeping and hurry up and wake up."

Pfft... Donna spat out the sweet iced tea she'd just drunk, but fortunately, the fried fish on the table had already been eaten.

At this moment, Klein sighed inwardly.

Even though I know it's nearly impossible to find the treasure--otherwise, the various Churches would've done so already with all their powerful Beyonders--I can't help getting excited when discussing such things. This is the charm of treasure! Even a mere legend can attract many adventurers!

Donna wiped her mouth and sat down like a lady, as though she wasn't the one who had lost her composure.

The little boy, Denton, asked, still eager for more, "Are there any other legends of treasure?"

Elland looked at Cleves, indicating that he should answer.

Cleves calmly drank a mouthful of black tea and said in a deep voice, "There are countless treasures at sea. The hidden lands of the elves, pirate ships lost in the depths of the fog, the underwater city sealed with powerful monsters, the last secret trove of Emperor Roselle, and so on."

Ah? The Emperor had already reached the level of leaving treasures and legends for others... If it's true, would there be Cards of Blasphemy among them? How many would there be? The legend of the Key of Death might help Mr. Azik regain more of his memories... Klein thought with curiosity and anticipation.

Chapter 497: The Bounty Wall

After their discussion of the treasure legends that had inspired generations of adventurers to take to the seas ended, the cook had also finished roasting the flesh of the murloc's belly.

The pieces had cooked to a milky white, with charred black patches, and were covered with tiny brown particles that glistened with a moist, oily sheen.

The layers of seasoning had seeped deep into the meat, giving it a tantalizing appearance.

"Desi grilled fish. It's unlike the versions you usually eat." Elland pointed at the white porcelain plate the cook set down.

Donna held her fork and knife and eagerly said, "I love honey-roasted fish!

"But this is also very appetizing."

Honey-roasted fish... How much honey would that take... If I have a chance to try it, the taste should be pretty good... Klein allowed his imagination to run wild.

With a chef, they had no need to serve themselves. They could only look on expectantly as pieces of fish were sliced, placed on different plates, and served in front of them.

Klein was very serious when it came to tasting delicacies, so he was in no hurry to eat the fish. Instead, he took a sip of black tea and used the faintly tart liquid to cleanse his palate of any remaining flavors.

After doing all of this, he forked a piece of fish and stuffed it into his mouth.

For an instant, he felt the piquant flavors of fennel, basil, and other spices. They awakened one taste bud after another.

Immediately afterward, the meat's delicious juices, the slightly astringent sea salt, and the refreshing sweet-and-sour taste of lemon all burst forth at once, filling his mouth and making it water.

As he chewed, the last of the fish's toughness yielded, its fat having been rendered away, revealing the meat's full richness and faint sweetness.

Swallowing the fish in his mouth, Klein recalled a food program he had watched in his previous life and selected the verdict that best matched the experience: "The layers of flavor are wonderfully distinct, excellent!"

"Haha, the way you speak makes you sound like a gourmet," Elland joked.

Donna waved her fork and echoed, "Uncle, perhaps you should write a newspaper column about restaurants and cuisines."

Eh, why didn't I think of this idea... That job would earn me money while letting me sample delicacies! The only problem is that an obese man can't be a flexible clown... Use the great art of vomiting on command? What a waste of food! Klein seriously considered Donna's suggestion.

"To a beautiful night!"

When there was very little food left, Elland poured himself some more of the Sonia blood wine and raised his glass with a ruddy face.

Klein and the others joined the toast in equally good spirits. "To a beautiful night."

They finished their drinks and watched the waiter clear the table and clean the deck.

They chatted for a while longer in the cold wind, discussing the mermaids that most interested Donna.

Cleves told the young lady that, in some legends, mermaids were also called Sirens, and they used their songs to bewitch humans not for entertainment, but to hunt them. Aside from the possibility of encountering these creatures on the sea route from the Gargas Archipelago to the depths of the Sonia Sea, they might also be found in dangerous waters unexplored by humans. However, all of this stemmed from the drunken boasting of certain pirates, and they all avoided the question of how they escaped from the mermaids' songs, rendering the claims highly suspect.

No matter what, this at least gives me a possible direction... Klein took note of what they had discussed.

"Donna, Denton, it's time to go back. You'll have to get up early tomorrow and have breakfast with your parents." Cecile looked at the moon's position.

"Fine." Donna stood up reluctantly.

Denton hurriedly asked, "D-do I have a chance at becoming an adventurer?"

His mind had been captivated by the previous hunt and the legends.

Cleves walked to his side, patted his shoulder, and said, "Before you ask this question, you need at least five years of combat training and study. I think your father will hire a good tutor for you."

"Yeah!" Denton's eyes lit up, and he nodded vigorously.

After five years, once you're an adult, you probably won't want to be an adventurer who could end up buried at the bottom of the sea at any time... Cleves's handling of the situation was very shrewd. He didn't refuse him outright, but instead gave him hope and let time erode his interest. That would prevent the child from suddenly becoming rebellious... Regardless, learning to fight is always beneficial... Klein slipped his hands into his pockets, impressed.

On the way back to the interior of the cabin, Cleves handed two five-pound notes to Klein.

"Your payment."

He had just received 150 pounds from Elland's purchase of the whole murloc.

"I didn't do anything," Klein instinctively refused.

Cleves glanced at him with his pale blue eyes and said in a low voice, "You freed Cecile to act and took good care of the children."

Took good care of the children? Klein was somewhat amused, but he finally took the two bills and drew a triangular Sacred Emblem on his chest.

"You're more generous than I thought. Thank you."

He stopped declining because he suddenly realized something. If he hadn't accepted the ten pounds, then in the eyes of a veteran adventurer like Cleves, it would appear as though he was dissatisfied with the price and was attempting to get more, as though he might attack them at any moment. Among self-proclaimed adventurers, there was definitely no shortage of greedy madmen!

Seeing Gehrman Sparrow pocket the money, Cleves looked away and said flatly, "This is a rule of the sea."

Without another word, he followed Cecile, Donna, and company into the cabin.

If I could get such rewards from every mission of this difficulty, then I would've made a fortune long ago as a private detective... Klein gave a self-deprecating laugh and turned his head to look at the red moon hanging high in the sky.

It still shone quietly and gently in the night.

Legends of the sea, different monsters... I've finally begun to feel like an adventurer. Klein turned around and walked to the edge of the ship. Bathing in the crimson veil, he admired the darkening waves. His mood gradually settled as he emerged from the gloom of Backlund's Great Smog.

The cold, damp wind slapped his face, and the vast, endless ocean filled his vision and opened his heart.

For a moment, Klein felt the urge to sing, but when he opened his mouth, he found that he couldn't remember any suitable modern lyrics.

I can't just say, "Oh sea, you're all water"... The Emperor's Savant Sequence is really well suited to such things. When I have the time, I should read through his poems so I don't look illiterate... Klein lampooned as he looked at the red moon and the ocean before sighing.

"What a beautiful night."

After accidentally losing their way, the exploratory team returned to the City of Silver.

Looking at the weed-filled cracks in the wall, Derrick found himself in a trance, as if he had been gone for many years.

Diagonally behind him, Demon Hunter Colin's eyes suddenly grew unfocused as he raised his hand to press on his right temple.

The rest of the team members were filled with heartfelt joy and relief.

After a difficult exploration, having a home waiting for them brought them the greatest happiness.

Colin's gaze returned to normal as he turned his head to the side and looked diagonally ahead.

Backlund, the White family.

After some thought, the confident Emlyn approached his parents and asked, "If I wanted to delve into the history of the Sanguine, who should I look for?"

If I were to ask about the City of Silver directly, it's very likely that I'll expose that something is wrong with me. Although I'm perfectly calm and unafraid, for the sake of the Ancestor and to save the entire Sanguine race, I have no choice but to hide it... I've always been interested in the history of the Sanguine. I've collected a lot of information, and Father and Mother know that, so they won't suspect anything... This excuse is perfect! Emlyn praised himself inwardly.

He resembled his father to a certain extent. He wore gold-rimmed glasses that made him look very professional.

The gentleman, who held a doctorate in medicine, put down the thick anatomy book in his hands, nudged his glasses, and said, "No one in Backlund knows more than Lord Nibbs."

... If I dared to seek out Lord Nibbs, I would've done so long ago... Emlyn thought of Mr. Fool's description of him as a messiah who had to bear the burden of a secret while being misunderstood, then pressed solemnly, "Aside from Lord Nibbs?"

"He's sleeping underground, so it's not convenient for him to be disturbed."

Emlyn's father pulled up the collar of his thick cotton pajamas and thought for a moment.

"Waymandy. He always thinks of himself as a historian."

Emlyn heaved a sigh of relief and smiled.

"I wish to visit him."

Woo!

The whistle sounded and the White Agate sailed into Damir Harbor.

It would replenish fresh food and water on the colonial island and set sail again the next morning.

After hunting the murloc, Klein spent the next two days in what could be described as leisurely boredom. He was thoroughly bored with the seascape, and he decided to head to the harbor's bar to look around that very night, hoping to get more information on mermaids and inspiration for his acting.

If I were to come across a pirate whose hands are stained with blood onshore, I wouldn't mind teaching him a lesson. There are still souls waiting to be released from Creeping Hunger... Klein's forehead beaded with sweat as he armed himself with all his mystical items as he left his cabin before heading down to the harbor.

On the way, he met Donna, Cleves, and the others. They seemed to be heading over to a port restaurant to taste Damir's most famous cured meat.

Donna and Denton greeted the adventurer, whom they had just gotten to know, without their parents noticing, and seemed curious as to where he was going.

Klein smiled back, pulled up his collar, and, following the directions of the sign, found the nearest bar.

Flying Fish & Wine... Klein looked at the signboard and saw that the outside of the bar was plastered with bounty notices.

Among them were the King of the Five Seas at 800,000 pounds and ordinary pirate captains worth just over a hundred. It was a unique display spanning the highest bounties to the lowest.

It's all money... Klein stood on the spot and looked at it for a long time.

He withdrew his gaze, pushed open the door, and entered the bar, only to find that it was abnormally quiet. It lacked the din customary in such places.

What happened? Klein looked around and saw Captain Elland, in a dark red coat, sitting at the bar, as well as two large men in the middle of the room, facing each other.

Chapter 498: Persona

One participant in the standoff was a brawny man in a blue-and-white striped shirt that marked him as a navy sailor. Despite the near-freezing temperature, his arms were bare.

He held a dagger against the throat of the man opposite him, but an ancient musket, a weapon fit for a museum, was aimed between his brows.

The musket's owner was also more than 1.8 meters tall, with strong muscles and an oily face. His head was shaved. A sea eagle tattoo adorned it, and he cursed, "Dogsh*t Navy sailor!

"No one in Damir Harbor would dare slander me as a pirate's informant!"

The navy sailor didn't yield to him, and both sides demonstrated the rich vocabulary of nautical profanity.

Klein observed for a few seconds, then he skirted the room to reach Captain Elland, who had a straight sword at his waist and a hidden musket on his body. He asked calmly and casually, "What happened?"

"A quarrel between two drunkards. In Damir Harbor and the surrounding waters, there have always been rumors that Sea Eagle Logan serves the master of the Black Tulip. The navy sailor said so a moment ago, and Sea Eagle just happened to hear him."

The master of the Black Tulip? Isn't that Admiral Hell Ludwell?

Klein turned and sat on a high stool in front of the bar counter. He tapped the wooden countertop.

"One glass of Southville beer."

"Six pence." A bronze-skinned bartender with pearly-white teeth wiped the cups as he said without enthusiasm.

When it comes to continental specialties, prices are much higher than in Backlund or Tingen... Klein fished out a handful of copper pence, counted six pence and gave it to him.

At this moment, Logan and the Navy sailor were stopped by the bar's bouncer. After they each traded threats, they retreated to different corners.

Perhaps it was because he had lost face, the Navy sailor hurriedly left after about ten seconds. The atmosphere in the bar became lively once again.

"Want to play some cards?" Captain Elland pointed to the stairs by the side of the bar.

"No." Klein's main purpose for coming was to gather information.

Elland subconsciously wanted to pat Klein on the shoulder, but Klein's cold, sharp demeanor stopped him. He could only pull back his hand and pretend to adjust his dark red coat, reminding him, "Don't touch the women here."

Klein nodded, picked up the glass of Southville beer, and took a swig.

"Also, don't trust anyone here. Only a small portion of what they say is true." Elland took his Lanti Proof and stepped onto the stairs that led to the second floor.

Klein turned his head to glance at him and asked without a change in expression, "Including you?"

"Perhaps." Elland was taken aback at first before he laughed out loud. "At least my reminder just now was real. Oh, and me being a man is true too!"

Not necessarily... There's a potion called Witch in this world... Klein looked away, drinking slowly and listening to the surrounding drinkers boasting.

Two or three minutes later, a short, thin man sat down next to Klein with his drink.

"Mate, you look like an adventurer." He tilted his head and smiled.

The man who greeted him had black hair, blue eyes, and prematurely aged features. He had a rather disreputable air.

"You could say that," Klein replied coldly.

"I can tell you're a hunter, a hunter who chases after bounties and riches." The short man looked around, lowered his head, and dropped his voice. "Have you heard of the Specter Empire?"

I've heard of Amway, and I've also heard of the Father in Heaven and the Messiah being sealed at the bottom of the sea... Using the power of Faceless, Klein made himself radiate unapproachability.

"Yes, a huge, ancient ghost ship full of treasures."

"We have clues to it!" the short man said in an infectious tone. "We found some information about where it will appear next! We don't want the pirates or the Navy to benefit from it, and we don't want to be robbed of our wealth, so we've decided to hire an armed merchant ship of our own to wait in that area, which would probably cost about 1,000 pounds. I've already found 15 companions and raised 720 pounds. Are you interested in participating?"

Without waiting for Klein to speak, he fumbled for a stack of brownish-yellow letters.

"I know you won't believe it so easily. In fact, no one would, but all fifteen friends who read this information decided to join our plan."

...Do I look that gullible? Or does every bumpkin who comes here encounter this sort of scam? While Klein was considering whether to appraise the letters, from the corner of his eye, he saw that Sea Eagle Logan, who had been quarreling earlier, was walking towards him.

"Woody, you're trying to scam someone again! You damn sewer rat!" Logan picked up the short man and flung him into the open space in the middle of the bar, where he sprawled on the floor.

The muscular man, with the bluish-green tattoo on his head, took Woody's seat and guffawed.

"Sorry, these are the rats of our Damir Harbor. They always do things that ruin our reputation.

"In fact, we're all very friendly. If you have anything you want to ask about, don't hesitate to ask me.

"Heh heh, don't believe what they say about me. I'm an upright person, and I have nothing to do with Admiral Hell!"

The more you emphasize that, the more suspicious it becomes... Klein's expression didn't change as he calmly said, "I want to know the latest rumors."

"No problem." Sea Eagle Logan slammed the bar top and said to the bartender, "Give me a plate of special cured meat. I'll be treating this mate to our most famous delicacy in Damir."

The bartender, while still maintaining his cold expression, pushed open the door and entered the kitchen. Soon, he brought out a plate of red and white, finely cut cured meat.

"Five pounds." He didn't look at Sea Eagle Logan, and instead looked straight at Klein.

"Five pounds." Sea Eagle Logan turned his head to the side, smiling warmly as he raised his arm to show his muscles. "Everyone just heard it. To thank me, you offered to treat me to some special cured meat."

For a moment, Klein failed to grasp what had happened. It was only when the bartender urged him a second time that he realized he was being blackmailed. Worse, their scam was rather well orchestrated.

First, they used an obvious farce to bring Sea Eagle Logan onto the scene and earn the target's goodwill. Then, under the pretense of treating the target, they ordered an exceptionally expensive plate of cured meat. Finally, Logan broke his word, reversed the story, and forced the purchase on him... It's no wonder that when Rat Woody was thrown out, those drunkards didn't make a sound... They were all afraid of Logan, who was rumored to be in the service of Admiral Hell... How should I deal with this? My present persona is Gehrman Sparrow, a somewhat crazy adventurer and bounty hunter... Klein raised his cup and drank a mouthful of the rich malt-flavored beer and said in his usual tone, "Why don't you just rob me?"

"Why don't I just rob you?" Logan was a little stunned by the question.

A moment later, he saw a fist expanding before his face.

Thump!

Klein's left fist landed on Sea Eagle Logan's chin, knocking him backward toward the bar counter.

With a push of his right palm, Klein nimbly left his chair and approached Logan's falling body.

He tensed his leg and drove his knee upward, slamming into Logan's lower abdomen.

Pfft! Logan threw back his head, his eyes bulging while his mouth hung half open.

Klein drew his gun, shoved the revolver into the man's mouth, and pulled the hammer back.

"I... I am..." Logan shouted indistinctly.

Klein looked him in the eye, pulled out his revolver, and swung it, striking the side of Logan's face with the butt of the gun.

Logan's teeth fell out one after another, and his mouth was stained with blood.

The heavy blow and overwhelming pain made his eyes roll back as he fainted.

Klein propped him up and pulled a handful of loose bills and coins out of his pocket.

A glance told him there was no more than five pounds. Klein threw the money on the bar and calmly said, "Keep the change."

The bartender's bronze face turned slightly pale as he shouted in panic, "My boss is White Shark!"

Without looking at him, Klein released his hand and allowed Sea Eagle Logan to fall to the ground. Then he sat down again, forked a piece of cured meat, and put it in his mouth. He found the taste rather unique as the flavors of the condiments unfurled in wisps, teasing his stomach and throat.

After eating two pieces, he looked up and asked, "Does your boss know that you're colluding with Sea Eagle?"

"No, he... No..." the bartender mumbled an answer.

Seeing that Klein had no intention of continuing his attacks and had paid the bill so quickly, the bouncers who had gathered nearby silently retreated.

Klein took a sip of his beer, glanced at Sea Eagle Logan on the ground, and calmly said to the bartender, "He's Ludwell's informant. How much is his bounty worth?"

"No, he's not." The bartender shook his head and said, "That's merely a claim he made up and spread himself. That Navy sailor from before was hired by him! That's the only way to make everyone here afraid of him..."

Upon hearing this answer, the drinkers at the bar all put down their glasses in surprise. Some of the drunkards even staggered over to Logan and spat in his face.

Pui! Pui! Pui! Many drinkers followed suit.

Klein lowered his head again and said while eating the special cured meat, "Tell me about the recent rumors."

The bartender breathed a sigh of relief as he wiped his glass and gave a piecemeal account of the rumors from the past two months. Klein had heard some of them before, but others were new to him.

The Imperial Navy's ironclad warship, Pritz, had destroyed a passing pirate crew during routine training... Panic over the behemoth's cannons had begun to spread among the small and medium-sized pirate powers... Some of them even wished to take advantage of the ironclad warship fleet's inchoate form to commit crimes in a frenzy before withdrawing from this trade with a sum of money... The sea wouldn't be peaceful for the next six months to a year... Admiral of Blood Senor and Vice Admiral Dusk Bulatov Ivan clashed in the southern waters of Sonia Island, and each of them lost two ships in the massive skirmish... Klein listened without asking questions, and he gradually filled his stomach.

After emptying the plate of cured meat and finishing his beer, he slowly stood up.

"Remember today's lesson." Klein handed the plate to the bartender.

The bartender was about to reach out when the hair at the back of his head was grabbed.

Bang!

Klein shoved him down hard, knocking the bartender's head against the bar top, sending splinters flying and blood gushing. The customers all tried to avoid the impact, and the bouncers rushed over quickly.

After doing all of this, Klein clapped his hands and picked up his own glass, meaning to pour the remaining beer onto the bartender's head.

One drop, two drops, three drops...

Klein silently gave up. He turned around and bent down, grabbed Sea Eagle Logan, and threw him to the bouncers who were rushing over.

Taking advantage of the bouncers' evasive movements and the chaos in the bar, Klein darted nimbly around them and easily left Flying Fish & Wine.

He pushed down his hat and hurried onward, turning towards a neighboring street.

After changing direction several times, he suddenly slowed his pace, and a gold coin appeared in his hand.

The gold coin kept weaving around in his fingers as though scouting his surroundings.

Chapter 499: Recruitment

Ding!

The gold coin flipped silently into the air, tumbled down, and landed squarely in the palm of Klein's hand.

Glancing down to see whether it showed heads or tails, Klein pivoted on his heel and smoothly turned into a quiet, dark alley.

The wind by the sea was cold and strong, and the air currents swirled, lifting his coat and causing his half top hat to almost fall off.

Suddenly, Klein stopped, turned around, and said in a deep voice, "Come out."

His eyes were sharp as he stared at the shadows around the corner.

After four or five seconds of silence, a figure emerged from the shadows. He chuckled and said, "Very sharp."

It was a man in a black cloak, about thirty years old. He had tawny eyebrows and bright, dark blue eyes. His features weren't sharply defined, as though he was from the region encompassing southern Intis, Lenburg, and Segar.

As soon as he saw him, a memory flashed through Klein's mind.

As he entered the Flying Fish & Wine, he had made a professional sweep of the room for anyone he needed to pay attention to.

At the time, this man had not stood out. The man had been drinking like a sailor and watching the commotion with casual curiosity. He was no different from the other guests, and his appearance wasn't distinctive, but his black cloak had left a certain impression on Klein, allowing him to recognize the man tailing him in an instant.

"What do you want?" Klein, who was maintaining his persona, crouched slightly, like a huge feline ready to pounce.

The man in the black cloak laughed once again.

"The fighting techniques and methods you displayed just now were exactly to my taste. I followed you to ask whether you're interested in joining us."

"Although that guy named Logan was indeed pretending to be an informant for Ludwell, White Shark Hamilton does have connections with many pirates. He's a character with a shady background, so he'll certainly take offense at your beating one of his men in the bar. There will be problems for you in the future, and I can help you resolve them.

"You're an adventurer, so you must dream of treasure. As for us, we're people who banded together to seek treasures such as the Specter Empire, Solomon's inheritance, the secret of the Fountain of Unaging, Death's Key, the Sunken Laurel, and Roselle's treasure while traveling the Five Seas. So far, although we haven't accomplished any of our main goals, we've found many missing pirate ships. Heh heh, what I said does sound like what that rat said, right?"

He cleared his throat and said, "Frankly speaking, we're a band of pirates formed from a group of adventurers, but we only plunder merchant and passenger ships when we're especially poor, and we don't harm the innocent. Our main focus is on finding treasures, and we often make profitable discoveries. And I kid you not, I once slept on a bed made of gold coins. If we run into any other pirate ships, a mere display of our prowess earns us compensation.

"By the way, our captain has decreed that before we recruit new people, we have to explain our creed and remuneration."

Creed? Your captain is a little interesting... Klein deliberately relaxed his posture to see if the enemy would take the opportunity to attack.

The black-cloaked man smiled in a relaxed manner.

"What I said before was our creed, and now, let me talk about the remuneration."

This fellow is quite confident... Although he wasn't a Spectator, Klein could tell that he was very confident and that he was completely unfazed by the scene in front of him.

"We don't have weekly salaries or annuities, but once we find treasure, or obtain riches from plundering, they'll be distributed according to our ranks. Under normal circumstances, when our luck holds, the lowest-ranking sailor can earn about two to three hundred pounds a year. I heard that this would make one a member of the middle class on land? Heh heh, if we find the Sunken Laurel, all of us will become tycoons!" the black-cloaked man explained casually. "According to our ranks, we would get different days off every month, but the leave must be accumulated and taken on a staggered schedule."

As he spoke, he suddenly cursed softly.

"Dogsh*t, just two years ago, we missed a good chance of finding the Specter Empire because Captain was on holiday!"

Pirates have annual leave? Klein found it somewhat surprising.

He could only feel the pirate crew's comic absurdity from the man's description, reminding him of the humorous Somali pirate recruitment advertisements he had seen in his previous life.

Seeing that Klein appeared shocked by his words, the black-cloaked man added with a smile, "As an adventurer, are you still chasing after the supernatural powers spoken of in legends?

"As long as you join us, you'll have a chance to possess them!"

Having said that, he coughed and said, "I forgot to introduce myself."

His expression turned solemn, his earlier jocularity gone.

"Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina Edwards's subordinate, Fourth Boatswain of the Golden Dream, Blazing Danitz."

After reporting his title, real name, and identity, Danitz waited patiently for the look of panic and fear to appear on Klein's face.

After a second, he heard the adventurer, who behaved somewhat madly despite his refined, polite appearance, say in a low voice, "Blazing Danitz with a bounty of 3,000 pounds?"

Danitz was about to reply when he had the illusion that the man standing in the dark alley had turned into an indescribable ravenous monster, salivating over his soul and flesh.

He suddenly clenched his fists; his body was no longer relaxed. He was so tense that he was trembling slightly.

His intuition told him that he was facing an abyss filled with madness and bloodlust!

In this state, Danitz had no idea how much time had passed before the other party spoke again. "Why are you here?"

"I-I'm on vacation..." Blazing Danitz's dignity rebelled at answering the question, but his instincts forced the words out.

As soon as he finished speaking, he felt the man retract his gaze, the hunger to gnaw on his flesh and soul disappearing.

He stood where he was and watched the young adventurer in a black coat and silk half top hat turn and walk to the other end of the alley. Just before rounding the corner, the adventurer stopped, looked back, and asked, "Where's White Shark?"

"H-he lives at 1 Sea Wall Avenue, but he spends most of his time on the second floor of the Flying Fish & Wine. It's no different today," Blazing Danitz answered truthfully.

Only when the figure disappeared into the distance did Danitz straighten his back and take off his cloak.

"What a terrifying fellow..." He sighed silently.

Following that, he collected his thoughts. I have to inform Captain that there's another terrifying person at sea.

This is a guy who looks like a gentleman on the outside but has the heart of a lunatic. If one doesn't have the determination and confidence to kill him, it's best not to deal with him.

Danitz pulled up the hood of his black cloak and decided to return to the hotel for some sleep. He planned to wait until the telegraph office opened the next morning before relaying the message to his intermediary in the Rorsted Archipelago.

As for what would happen to White Shark, he didn't care at all.

In the corner of another quiet street, Klein stood in the shadows, looking down at the black glove on his left hand.

He found that, although Creeping Hunger had been sealed by Mr. Azik, its inherent thirst for flesh and souls remained and was trying to manifest itself.

Under normal circumstances, Klein wasn't worried that the Sealed Artifact would cause any problems, but when he had the urge to kill someone, its influence was enough to release the hunger that might recoil upon him.

Earlier, when he heard that Danitz was a famous pirate on the bounty list, his desire to hunt instantly gave rise to killing intent, causing Creeping Hunger to become as lively as a fish in water.

Fortunately, Klein always had excellent self-control in this regard. From Danitz's words, he was able to judge that the pirate wasn't steeped in sin, so he easily restrained his impulse.

With Creeping Hunger, Gehrman Sparrow's persona will be flawless... Klein paused for a few seconds, took out a gold coin, and performed two divinations. First, he divined whether Blazing Danitz was lying, and second, whether White Shark Hamilton could harm him.

The first revelation showed that there was no need for Blazing Danitz to lie, and the second one showed that White Shark Hamilton was unable to cause harm to him.

Klein put away the gold coin, pressed down his hat, and while doing so, he swept his palm down and touched his face.

He instantly changed his appearance--blond hair, blue eyes, and ordinary facial features!

Then, Klein unbuttoned his overcoat and pulled out his shirt so that it was no longer tucked into his trousers.

After this simple change in attire, Klein, who didn't carry a cane, appeared sweaty and dry-lipped as he identified the direction and headed back to the Flying Fish & Wine bar!

On the way, he met Blazing Danitz again. The man only glanced at him once before withdrawing his gaze and heading for the hotel opposite the bar.

After inspecting the bounty wall, Klein calmly reached out with his hand, pushed open the door, and walked in.

At this moment, it had been less than ten minutes since he escaped.

In the bar, most of the customers had dispersed, but there were still many drunkards gathered here to watch the show.

Their eyes swept over the new guest, but they all looked away soon after, and Klein made his way unimpeded to the bar counter.

He saw the bartender standing fearfully beside a fat man. The bartender's forehead was swathed in white bandages, his nostrils were stuffed with tissue, and his face was bruised.

The fat man was tall and broad. His skin was fair and he looked like a big white shark that had swum ashore.

He touched his shiny bald head and said to Elland, who was wearing a dark red jacket and a straight sword, "I heard you know that guy.

"There are only three passenger ships that docked today, so there won't be many unfamiliar outsiders. Don't try to lie!"

Elland patted the hilt of his sword and smiled casually.

"Yes, he is my passenger.

"But the problem today clearly stemmed from your people."

"So I just want him to come back here, apologize to me, and compensate me for the damage done to the bar," the large, pale fat man said with a frown.

Elland laughed and said, "White Shark, I have a proverb from my hometown: 'Don't hate the wild dogs passing by because of the rats in your storehouse.'"

"Just Elland, is that your answer?" White Shark Hamilton narrowed his eyes.

Elland gripped the wooden handle of the musket, took a step forward, and said in a deep voice, "Yes, that's my answer!"

Captain sure has his way of doing things... Klein was slightly surprised at Elland's response.

After staring at each other for a few seconds, White Shark Hamilton drew a deep breath.

"You've helped me before, so I don't need an apology. However, he must compensate half of my losses, and you will be the one to pass them on."

"Good suggestion." Elland smiled.

White Shark Hamilton's face darkened as he looked around.

He suddenly threw out his hand, slapping the bartender in the face.

The bartender flew out, all his teeth dropping to the floor.

Klein quietly watched from five meters away, as if none of this had anything to do with him.

Chapter 500: Interrogating White Shark

Bam! The bartender fell to the ground, writhing in pain.

White Shark Hamilton snorted without saying anything. He turned around and walked toward the second floor, stepping on the creaking wooden stairs.

Once the excitement ended, the drunkards dispersed one by one. Unperturbed, Captain Elland and the others returned upstairs to continue playing cards.

Klein took the opportunity to follow.

He returned to Flying Fish & Wine not to deal with White Shark, who posed no threat to him, but simply to obtain more information from the bar's owner, who was connected to many pirate factions. After all, he had named his new identity Gehrman, which carried a secret implication: hunting bloodstained pirates. He planned to use their souls, flesh, and Beyonder characteristics to replace the souls within Creeping Hunger that awaited release.

There was no gas in Damir Harbor, and the corridor on the second floor was quite dark. The brass candleholders set into the walls burned dimly, their flames flickering.

Klein observed the surroundings as he wiped his face, silently transforming into one of the bouncers on the first floor.

He used Illusion Creation to compensate for his mismatched attire.

After finishing his preparations, he walked toward the room that his spiritual intuition identified as White Shark Hamilton's.

He passed the card room without attracting attention.

He stopped in front of the bouncers who were guarding the corridor and said in a low voice, "There's something going on downstairs again."

"Holy Lord of Storms, what's happening tonight?" a bouncer sighed.

"I hope those women don't get hurt," another guard said worriedly.

He was referring to the prostitutes who conducted their business in the bar.

"They're fine." Klein stepped past the bouncers and knocked on White Shark's door.

"Who is it?" Hamilton asked warily.

"Boss, it's me. Something happened downstairs again!" Klein recalled what he had learned while observing the earlier commotion and deliberately hoarsened his voice.

"Damn it!" Hamilton bellowed. "Come in and explain to me what happened!"

Klein turned the doorknob and walked in.

When he closed the door, he dispelled the illusion, and the muscles on his face rapidly squirmed, returning him to his previous identity: the new customer with blond hair, blue eyes, and ordinary facial features.

"You..." Hamilton was stunned for a moment before he immediately opened his mouth wide in an attempt to shout loudly.

At the same time, many illusory fish scales surfaced on the back of his hand, his already large, fat body swelling.

Suddenly, his heart began to beat faster, and a powerful, instinctive fear seized him by the throat.

At this moment, he felt that the stranger standing by the door was a demon that had been starved for many days, regarding his flesh and soul with an icy, ravenous gaze.

All of a sudden, White Shark Hamilton was seized by such extreme panic that he could not react effectively.

Klein slowly walked to the sofa and sat down. He smiled politely.

"Now, can we talk calmly?"

The feeling of being stared at by a horrifying monster suddenly disappeared. Hamilton relaxed abruptly while his body shrank like a punctured balloon.

He didn't recklessly call for help. Beads of sweat covered his forehead as he asked, "Who are you? What do you want?"

"A hunter," Klein answered casually. "I heard that you're connected to a number of pirate factions. I would like to know details about each of them."

"No, I'm not..." White Shark Hamilton denied reflexively.

He immediately sensed that extreme hunger again, as if the man's eyes had taken on a dark red hue.

Klein considered his persona inwardly and said with a gentlemanly smile, "You have two choices.

"First, answer frankly. Second, let me kill you, then answer honestly."

Kill me and channel my spirit? White Shark Hamilton had heard of similar rumors. He swallowed hard and asked, "Why do you want to know about any of this?"

Klein smiled and answered, "I'm a hunter, so I'm hunting bounties."

Hamilton suddenly felt that the man's polite smile was tinged with an indescribable madness, and he couldn't help but blurt out, "Are... Are you mad?

"I've seen many similar adventurers, but all of them have been buried at the bottom of the sea!

"It's not hard to kill a lone pirate, but can you defend against any further retaliation? The whores in the bar or the seemingly ordinary customers might all be pirate informants! Your friendly accomplice could be bribed at any moment and you'll be shot in the back! The pirates will gather information in advance and surround your ship. Can you protect all the passengers? Can you survive a cannon bombardment? On the sea where there's no room to run, how are you going to survive?"

After venting his terror in one breath, he saw the man, who called himself a hunter, give a gentle, kindly smile.

"Just kill them all, and then there won't be such a problem."

A true madman... White Shark Hamilton immediately took a deep breath and said, "I'm in contact with many pirates, but it's a passive relationship. They need to dispose of the cash, jewelry, and goods they plunder in exchange for alcohol, food, fresh water, weapons, and the company of women. They have to do that through me, but I can only wait here for them. I don't know where their ships sail or where they're going."

"What else?" Klein asked calmly.

His reply just now was mainly to scare White Shark. As for pirate retaliation, he wasn't worried at all. As a Faceless, he might as well find a place to drown himself at the bottom of the sea if they could find him so easily.

And... White Shark Hamilton's throat bobbed, but he gave neither an affirmative nor a negative response.

He clamped his mouth shut and looked at the gentleman in the half top hat. The gentleman's eyes were calm and reserved, as though madness was brewing within him.

The uneasy silence resembled the calm sea before a storm, with hidden currents colliding and gathering force.

Finally, Hamilton shifted his gaze away and slapped a hand on the desk in frustration.

"Yes, I'm still gathering information for them. If I learn anything urgent, I'll use the radio transceiver they gave me to alert them."

White Shark didn't dare take the risk. He was afraid that the man possessed unique Beyonder powers that could determine whether he was speaking truthfully and telling the whole truth.

"Radio transceiver?" Klein, who had won his gamble, immediately seized on the term.

"That's what they called it when explaining it to me. It's like a telegram, but it doesn't require a wire." Hamilton turned and walked over to the gray safe and squatted down.

A wireless telegraph? The pirates possess such advanced technology? Klein could vaguely guess what the radio transceiver was.

He had previously thought of inventing something like this, but when he flipped through relevant magazines, he realized that wireless telegraphs had long since appeared. However, they had not found their place in the commercial world. The Berserk Sea, which separated the Northern and Southern Continents with its constant thunder and lightning, chaotic magnetic fields, and violent storms, made only a few sea routes accessible. Even when ships were equipped with wireless telegraphs, the devices were almost useless. Similarly, the weather in the Fog Sea and Sonia Sea changed drastically, and numerous factors affected electromagnetic transmission. The use of wireless telegraphy had been severely restricted.

Could it be that there's an enhanced model that can solve some of those problems? Klein watched as White Shark pried open the floor plank in front of the safe and twisted a mechanism to reveal a secret door in the wall.

Behind the secret door was a hidden cabinet with three levels. On the top level were some documents and bills, a revolver, a new type of half-arm gun, and other weapons, while the bottom layer was filled with complicated black machinery.

With just a glance, Klein deduced from his previous life's impressions and the information he had gathered that the machine was a radio transceiver.

"They call it a radio transceiver. Its transmissions can be received by similar devices as far away as the Rorsted Archipelago. Any farther and it'll depend on the weather and one's luck. Usually, it's cumbersome and severely limited." Hamilton didn't know much about the machine, so he described its capabilities vaguely based on his experience and instructions.

It's better than the new radio transceivers that are now being commercialized... I wonder who invented it... Klein quietly listened and asked, "Who are they?"

He made himself look like a bounty hunter who didn't understand technology.

White Shark Hamilton wiped the cold sweat from his forehead and said, "Silver Coin Viper Oder, who claims to serve the owner of the Dawn, and Old Quinn, Admiral of Blood's intelligence officer. They appeared together, and I can't be certain whether they're working together. Of course, Oder has never offered more than claims."

The owner of the Dawn, that Queen Mystic? Klein looked away, a gold coin appearing in his hand.

The gold coin kept weaving between his fingers before finally leaping into the air and landing. White Shark watched in puzzled trepidation.

Glancing down at it, Klein slowly stood up.

At this moment, he suddenly asked, "Who gave you the potion?"

"O-Old Quinn..." Hamilton hesitated but still chose to answer honestly.

Klein nodded and asked nothing further. He turned and walked toward the door.

Thud! The wooden door opened and closed. The figure wearing a black coat disappeared from White Shark's room.

Hamilton held his breath, waited for more than ten seconds, and finally let out a long sigh.

He quickly wiped the sweat from his face, placed the radio transceiver on the desk, flipped through a codebook, and hurriedly sent a telegram:

"I was targeted!

"By an unfamiliar fellow!"

Beside the completely engrossed Hamilton, Klein had his hands in his pockets as he quietly watched, memorizing the full frequency band and the codes.

His apparent departure had been nothing more than a large-scale illusion, more than enough to deal with a Low-Sequence Beyonder on the Sailor pathway like White Shark.

As for whether he could recall the details later, a Seer had no need to worry. A dream divination was enough to recall everything.

Admiral of Blood and his men delight in killing and bloodshed. They subject women to brutal violence. Every time they rob a passenger ship, they leave tragedy behind... This is common knowledge, and they themselves are proud of it. They never hesitate to boast about it... The target of the hunt and the risk involved--I'll give priority to them... Klein thought for a moment and then prepared to leave the room while Hamilton tidied and put away the radio transceiver.

He didn't plan on dealing with White Shark for the time being, as he was afraid of disturbing the real prey. Anyway, a man like him, fixed to a territory on land, could easily be dealt with later by sending a letter exposing his crimes.

Klein's soundless footsteps caused the door to slowly open and then quietly close, letting in a faint, cool breeze.
